#6860. Before & After: Two Best Friends Turned a Stripped-Down Tudor Into a Colorful, Antique-Filled Dream Home

Designer Anne Hulcher Tollett understood her client’s signature aesthetic better than anyone.

Anne Hulcher Tollett can’t remember a life without her best friend, Van. They met in a playpen as toddlers and quickly became an integral part of each other's lives, with many childhood memories taking place in Van’s house growing up. Tollett has been with her best friend through every milestone, including as her maid of honor at her wedding to her husband, Sean, and when the couple put an offer on a suburbanized Tudor home in Richmond, Virginia.

The five-bedroom house had good bones and an intuitive flow, but it needed help getting some of its charm back. When Van and Sean bought it for their family of four (plus their dog, Jelly Bean, and a small flock of chickens), it was whitewashed and lacked architectural character. In short, it wasn’t up to par with Van’s eclectic style, according to Tollett, but as the principal designer of Hanover Avenue, she reassured her bestie that she could easily take it from suburban chic to warm, layered, and personality-filled without having to completely rework the interior.

“Van’s aesthetic is diametrically opposed to ‘simple’ or ‘builder-grade,’ and although this house had been meticulously renovated and maintained by the previous owners, some of the charm she loves had been removed,” says Tollett. The designer and her team focused on lighter renovations and cosmetic upgrades, walking her best friend through every decision, from wallpaper choices to reworking the staircase to highlight the amazing treasures Van has collected on her travels over the years.

Throughout the renovation, Tollett and Van leaned into their sisterly shorthand, resulting in a project with very few disruptions. “Our biggest challenge was creating unexpected quirky moments without taking down walls or undoing all of the thoughtful and well-built upgrades that came with the house,” Tollett explains. “We found places on tables, walls, and bookshelves to highlight everything Van and her family have collected over the years. It makes the house feel like theirs and not the product of some design firm.”

Walls were covered with grasscloth, patterns were layered on throw pillows and vintage rugs, and anything that wasn’t antique or pre-owned was chosen to add a touch of patina to the space, allowing Van’s collections to truly sing.

Once Tollett was finished with the renovation, the house told the story of their friendship and a life well lived. Because the designer knows Van like the back of her hand, she was able to create a retreat that feels like a true reflection of who she is and how her family lives without editing out any of Van’s eccentricities. “We understood her design sensibility and took something well-built but fairly plain and made it fun, pretty, and interesting at every turn, which is exactly how anyone would describe Van!” Tollett adds. Besides, who better to design your house than the person who knows you better than yourself?

FAST FACTSDesigner: Anne Hulcher Tollett of Hanover AvenueLocation: Richmond, VirginiaThe Space: A 4,600-square-foot Tudor home with five bedrooms, four full bathrooms, and two half-baths

The barrel back and turquoise chairs were finds that Tollett sourced for Van’s previous home, proving that timeless profiles and accessible sizes can work in many different spaces throughout their lifetimes.

“A room like this with a vaulted ceiling and white walls creates a wonderful backdrop for bold pops of color and patterns because it can visually hold its own against furnishings with personality,” she says. “Van had the beautiful sideboard, and we loved pairing it with the tattooed table.”

Possibly one of the most remarkable makeovers in the entire house, Tollett coated the entryway in deep blue paint, topping it off with darling wallpaper on the ceiling for added dimension. “This is a great example of how a can of paint, a roll of wallpaper, and some strategically placed treasures can transform a room,” she says.

Large-scale grasscloth wallpaper instantly gave the once-all-white box character and rhythm, setting a serene backdrop for the rest of the unique finds in this dining room. The chandelier hanging over the table had long been on Tollett’s list to use in a project, and Van’s home was the perfect spot.

“The dining room is right near the front door, so it’s hung to look like it's being blown by the entrance breeze—a true classic with a twist!” the designer says.

Above the antique bar (whose natural patina plays so well with the wallpaper), are two framed photos of puppets, which Van found during one of her globe-trotting trips. She carried them around in her backpack, and Tollett loved the story behind them so much that she had them framed. On the opposite wall, behind the piano, is a portrait of Van’s grandmother in a vibrant green dress.

The kitchen was well-maintained when Tollett got her hands on it, but it didn’t feel all that fun, and it needed more than simple cosmetic refreshes like other areas of the home.

“We removed the big-box island lighting and reworked the storage, hood, and casework on the range wall,” Tollett explains. “We did the same on the wall opposite the range,” she adds, which was in fantastic shape, so they kept it.

To give the cook space more character, the designer installed a custom Delftware tile backsplash. The island was also in good shape, so Tollett upgraded the hardware, faucet, face frames, and lighting above it, opting for three Pinch pendant lights which bring an interesting, natural texture to the space.

“Open concept kitchens are tricky because they are seen from every angle, so we knew we needed to improve the storage to hide clutter,” she adds. Opposite the range wall, Tollett removed the open shelves, creating closed casework instead and topping everything off with pretty hardware and colors that play well with the adjacent dining and living rooms.

Tollett knew she was going to wrap this powder room in a beautiful butterfly-and-bird wallpaper, so she custom-matched a paint to fully drench the room in a whimsical emerald green hue. They even had their wonderful wallpaper installers cut out a few extra birds and butterflies to extend the design onto the ceiling, creating a more immersive experience. “We wanted the powder room to pack an equal punch to the bold, blue entryway it connects to,” she explains.

One of the mantras Tollett and her team at Hanover Avenue follow is “wonderfully weird,” which the hand sculpture extending through the wall to hold the soap perfectly captures. “The space feels not only more fun but also much more personalized to our clients!”

During the renovation, Van’s mother passed away, so Tollett transformed this almost-forgotten area into an homage to the matriarch. “We reused her beautiful plate rack and hung all her flawless collections of plates on the walls,” the designer says, officially turning this room, which overlooks the vegetable gardens, into the “plate room.”

They papered the walls in warm neutral grasscloth and hung relaxed Roman shades to softly filter the natural light, furnishing the room with many of the fabulous pieces Van already owned (and loved). “This is a wonderful example of ‘reimagining what you have,’ and finding ways to remember the ones we love in our own rooms,” Tollett adds. “Van’s mom was the coolest!”

“Decades of sleepovers in Van’s childhood bedroom allowed me to execute exactly what she was hoping to achieve,” Tollett explains. Though there are many layers of pattern in this sitting area, the designer managed to prevent it from feeling busy by keeping the same serene color palette for most of the textiles, minus a few pops of red for a warm balance.

“This room is a grown-up translation of Van’s entire design sensibility that began when she was young and was nurtured in her adulthood during her travels,” Tollett adds.

On the other side of the room is a calming wash of warm white paint and natural textures, giving Van a visual place of rest ideal for sleeping. Tollett calls it a “well-collected, well-traveled, layered space that seems to have evolved all on its own—a sanctuary for Van and Sean,” which makes the designer especially happy since she was the maid of honor at the couple’s wedding.

The primary bath really only needed some cosmetic upgrades, but they did a lot of heavy lifting. Airy Roman shades soften the space while Van’s mother’s bench and one of her existing rugs give it personality. By opting for two warmer paint colors and swapping out key fixtures, such as the lights, hardware, and faucets, Tollett created a serene, sophisticated escape that looks completely new, all with the same bones.

Tollett tapped into her childhood memories with her best friend when designing this room. “During slumber parties, Van and I fell asleep across from each other in twin beds, so it felt fitting to create another bedroom that fosters the same kind of childhood chatter,” she says, adding that stepping inside is like stepping into a time machine going back to 1983.

Along with the beds, much of the look was directly inspired by Van’s childhood bedroom, the round green pillow on the bed having been plucked directly from it. The pre-existing Tudor windows were perfect for the vintage vibe.

Tucked away at the far end of the home is a spot connected to the sunroom lounge. Dubbed the “dayroom”—an informal spot dedicated to relaxation and reading—Tollett wanted it to feel cozy and as collected as the rest of the home but still a bit private. They washed the walls in a soft gray-green hue and did the same to the custom day bed, finishing off the room with antique bookcases in a complementary gray tone. It’s easy for small rooms like these to feel overcrowded, but the pale color palette takes away some visual weight.

As founder and principal designer of Hanover Avenue, Anne Hulcher Tollett is intimately involved in every project the Richmond-based design firm takes on, from high-end residential to boutique commercial. Tollett finds a thrill in uncovering her clients’ design styles and endless joy in creating their dream homes that reflect who they are.

#6858. It’s Time to Take Full Stock of Bond 26

Robert Pattinson is the new 007, with Denis Villeneuve directing and Steven Knight writing. From the villain to a possible release date, here’s what we know about Bond’s next chapter.

The search for the new James Bond was one of Hollywood’s most closely scrutinized casting processes. Producer Amy Pascal said the team was being “really, really methodical” in its search for Craig’s successor, and the shortlist of hopefuls was as long as Bond’s list of ex-flings, with about the same survival rate.

Josh O’Connor was an early favorite until he stepped back in October, saying he planned a career break in 2027; the Daily Mail later reported that he had turned the role down. Elsewhere, the betting and the gossip circled Slow Horses heartthrob Jack Lowden, Jacob Elordi, James Norton, Jack O’Connell, and Masters of the Air’s Callum Turner.

But after all that Bond-esque bluffing and counterbluffing from Amazon MGM Studios, Robert Pattinson was unveiled as the new 007 in mid-November. Everybody (mostly) breathed a sigh of relief—and turned their attention to the rest of the upcoming reboot. So, what do we know so far?

The second crucial element of Le Nouveau Bond is that French-Canadian director Denis Villeneuve will direct. He, too, has pedigree, not least his epic three-part Dune series, the first two films of which have already grossed more than $1 billion worldwide, with Part Three due out in December. “If there’s one thing we can be sure of with Denis, it’s that he will do something interesting with this,” a source within the film industry told me. “He is one of those directors who can turn even a bad script into a good film.”

Speaking of which, the other major name already signed up to the reimagining is Peaky Blinders creator Steven Knight. He will write the story.

Knight seems a natural fit for a darker, more character-driven Bond. His breakout was the screenplay for Dirty Pretty Things, which earned him an Oscar nomination. He went on to write David Cronenberg’s brutal Eastern Promises and the one-man thriller Locke.

But it was Peaky Blinders that made his name: a stylized, violent, brooding saga of a damaged man at the rotten heart of a criminal empire, which became a global hit for the BBC and Netflix. Knight, in other words, has previous when it comes to writing moody, morally murky men.

No need to say much—we know what we’re dealing with here. A man who makes good choices. A star.

At forty, Pattinson is a little older than Craig was when he got the job in 2005, aged thirty-seven. But the career behind him is hardly that of a conventional leading man. After the teen-idol years of Twilight, he spent a decade deliberately steering away from the obvious: Cronenberg’s Cosmopolis, the Safdie brothers’ Good Time, Robert Eggers’s The Lighthouse, and Claire Denis’s High Life, before returning to the blockbuster fold with Christopher Nolan’s Tenet, and The Batman.

He’s made stellar choices throughout his career, turning a heartthrob start into a credible run of good, risk-taking roles.

The first, most critical drop will, of course, be the villain. Because, as they say in Hollywood, a movie is only ever as good as its villain. Without a dastardly supervillain for Bond to shoot his PPK at, Bond is just a red flag in a black suit. No names have yet reared their heads. But that didn’t stop us from suggesting a few candidates.

Speaking of which, Amazon has shown no indication of a plan to depart from the Bond-girl tradition. Though it’s (thankfully) likely to be handled sensitively for 2026: expect a complex and fully realized love interest who will still be in a gorgeous dress by the second act.

Margaret Qualley has emerged as the bookies’ favorite, having already taken part in a Bond-tribute dance at the 2025 Oscars. Sydney Sweeney has said she’s up for joining the franchise, though she told Variety she’d have more fun playing Bond himself.

Anya Taylor-Joy, Florence Pugh, and Zendaya have also been linked, with the obvious advantage of having worked with Villeneuve on Dune.

They were most recently played, respectively, by Ralph Fiennes, Ben Whishaw, and Naomie Harris. It’s not known whether any of them will return to the roles, though history suggests it’s likely.

Craig’s run offers a rough template, for what it’s worth (which is not much). He was unveiled in October 2005, started shooting Casino Royale the following January, and had it in cinemas by November 2006. Pattinson’s unveiling comes just a few weeks later in the calendar, so a similar timetable would see cameras rolling early next year and a potential release date around the end of 2027. Though that feels like a tight turnaround for such a gargantuan project. Amazon will want to get this right, so given how long it strung out Pattinson’s casting, 2028 seems a more likely ballpark.

Here’s a boring-but-pertinent fact: three of Daniel Craig’s five James Bond films debuted in October: Quantum of Solace, Skyfall, and Spectre. The remaining two, Casino Royale and No Time to Die, were released in November and September, respectively.

But this is, of course, guesswork—Amazon has offered no indication that it will follow the Craig timeline, or anything else about the movie’s schedule.

This is perhaps the most interesting part of the whole process. It began in February 2025, when the Broccoli dynasty (led by long-serving Bond masterminds Barbara Broccoli and Michael G. Wilson) announced they were handing over creative control to Amazon MGM Studios (which was formed when Amazon bought Bond’s parent studio in 2022).

Within hours of the announcement, Amazon’s owner, Jeff Bezos—the world’s richest man—took to X to ask the world: “Who’d you pick as the next Bond?”

This followed rumors of a rift between the two parties, with the Wall Street Journal reporting that Barbara suggested to friends that she didn't like the idea of Amazon execs taking a data-driven approach to the series, as well as the use of the word “content” in creative meetings.

Just as Disney+ has produced many Star Wars spin-offs, Amazon MGM is expected to do the same with Bond—a process that began last year with the first TV series, 007: Road to a Million, a game show hosted by Succession’s Brian Cox.

Mike Hopkins, head of Prime Video and Amazon MGM Studios, said in February 2025: “We are honored to continue this treasured heritage, and look forward to ushering in the next phase of the legendary 007 for audiences around the world.”

#6855. How a Connected Home Supports a Good Night’s Rest

How a wellness content creator uses Hubspace from The Home Depot to curate an environment for better sleep.

As a certified holistic health coach and wellness content creator, Carolina Salazar knows the importance of a peaceful wind-down routine before bed. She trusts Hubspace from The Home Depot that powers many of the smart devices in her home, to help ease her into a good night’s sleep.

Hubspace, The Home Depot’s exclusive smart home platform, powers more than 250 products across select private brands—like Hampton Bay, Husky, and Vissani—as well as national brand partners. It effortlessly connects everyday essentials, including smart security, indoor and outdoor lighting, appliances, switches, watering controls, and more.

Some of the Hubspace app’s most practical features help users personalize lighting and improve home security. Hubspace Connect, for example, lets you control compatible lighting from a single switch, while Vacation Mode is a security feature that turns lights and smart plugs on and off while you’re away. One of Salazar’s favorite features is Circadian Rhythm, which automatically transitions lights from cooler, whiter tones to warmer ones as the day goes on.

“I can set my lights to follow my circadian rhythm, so they shift automatically throughout the day,” says Salazar. “And when they start to turn warm in the evening, that’s my cue to slow it down.” That can mean cozying up with a book, brewing a cup of hot tea, or, in her case, lighting a candle at her bedside and journaling. When Salazar is finished putting her thoughts to paper, she uses the Hubspace app to adjust the color, temperature, and brightness of her lights, dimming them as she gets closer to sleep. This can even be programmed automatically in order to reduce screen time before bed.

“Colder rooms bring better sleep, so I also have Hubspace turn on my AC at night,” she says.

The Hubspace app is free to download and easily syncs with your home’s smart devices. You can enable voice control for screen-free commands—also a plus if you’re trying to reduce your pre-sleep screen time. And the app runs on both WiFi and Bluetooth—so even if the WiFi is down, your nighttime routine can still run seamlessly.

#6854. Dancing with the Stars Judge Carrie Ann Inaba on the ‘Healing’ Power of Movement and Why She Takes Risks

In ELLE’s Office Hours series, we ask people in powerful positions about their first jobs, worst jobs, and everything in between. This month, we sat down with Dancing with the Stars judge and dancer Carrie Ann Inaba. For the uninitiated, Inaba has been the sole female judge on the hit dance competition show since its inception, but her ambitions in entertainment began long before she arrived in the ballroom. After being discovered at a talent show in Hawaii, Inaba moved to Japan to pursue her dreams of performing, a leap of faith that catapulted her career to new heights. From choreographing and staging shows like American Idol and So You Think You Can Dance to bringing ballroom dance into the homes of millions, her career has been full of big risks and what she calls the “healing art” of dance. Ahead of the season 35 finale of Dancing with the Stars, Inaba reflects on how she got her start in the biz and the moment that changed everything in her career, then pulls back the curtain on what makes Dancing with the Stars a true feat of live television.

My very first job was working for my father when I was about 10 or 11 years old. He was an entrepreneur and, at the time, a real estate developer with an office in the heart of Waikiki. After school or on weekends, I would go to his office, and he would always put me to work. I think those early experiences are why I’ve always had such a positive association with work.

I was “discovered” by a Japanese producer at a talent show in Hawaii called Search for Talent. I was dancing with my friend Tina Horii, and we were offered a recording contract with Pony Canyon Records, with the intention of eventually returning to the U.S. as crossover artists. At the last moment, she decided not to go, but I felt compelled to honor the commitment. As part of my recording deal, I negotiated a contract to perform as a dancer on one of Japan’s most popular television shows, Yoru no Hit Studio DX. My love of dancing was still my first priority. I loved that chapter of my life. I was a member of the Dancing Dolls, much like the Solid Gold Dancers or the troupe on Dancing with the Stars. We performed alongside the musical guests, and it was a dream come true. My recording career was more challenging. I was uncomfortable being the center of attention, and everything felt rushed. It felt more like a business than a creative endeavor. The day after I performed my first single, “Party Girl,” on Fuji TV, I was mobbed by fans in Harajuku. It was an incredible introduction to the world of live television, which has become a thread throughout my career.

When I booked Dancing with the Stars, suddenly an entirely new world of opportunities opened up to me, and I wasn’t necessarily ready for all of them. Until then, I had been very intentional about the direction of my career. I was focused on dance, choreography, and producing. Everything had dance at its center. I had produced some live shows but really wanted to produce television. So the job of judging on Dancing with the Stars felt like a good fit toward that goal. What [the show] gave me was the opportunity to learn my own voice.

At 18, I moved to Japan, a country where I didn’t speak the language. I asked to be released from my recording contract when I realized the direction of my career was no longer aligned with who I was. I’ve never been afraid to walk away from an opportunity when staying would mean compromising my values.

Later, I chose to accept the job of opening Madonna’s Girlie Show Tour by doing something that had never been done before: descending a 50-foot unsecured fire pole with a non-weight-bearing safety. It was basically a live stunt that I performed every night of that tour. I had to go through intense training for it. It was controversial, but I really liked the idea of it and the statement it was making, so I said yes. It was one of the scariest things I’ve ever done; there was real risk every time I got on that pole. But it was very rewarding and I believed in what I was doing. As an Asian American woman, I wanted to break down stereotypes of what we could do.

I also think I made a very bold move this year by standing up for myself amid some workplace drama. I’m still processing what happened, and since the competition is still going on, I will leave it at that. But it was a profound moment for me in my career and life.

To trust myself, trust my choices, and always speak my truth no matter how uncomfortable it is to do so. I live by that.

It’s so much more than just steps and technique—it is a healing art form that empowers people. I encourage and notice the aspects outside and around the technique because that is what I know to be important from my personal and professional experiences. After a dance is done, most people will not remember the steps themselves or how many heel leads they had or didn’t have; they will remember how the dance made them feel. Of course, they need to do the correct style we have asked of them and follow any rules we have established, but we aren’t creating professional dancers to compete in Blackpool. We are an entertainment show that really celebrates the human spirit and the courage to try something new. That is what makes our show so special.

I can personally say that in 35 seasons, I have never been told what score to give a performance. Not once. I don’t think I would be able to continue judging if that were ever to happen. It would go against my core values and everything I believe about the responsibility of being a judge.

I don’t think people fully understand the scope of what we accomplish each week. The sheer amount of creative and physical energy it takes to bring this show to life is extraordinary. Every week, we essentially make the impossible possible. From choreography, performance, production, storytelling, and costume design to social media strategy, brand partnerships, publicity, security, catering, and transportation, there are so many moving parts. And once we start that first episode, it never stops until the Tuesday before Thanksgiving. It’s a feat on its own, but it’s also that we do it live every week.

There are no do-overs. Live television is high-stakes and high-pressure, and Dancing with the Stars is unlike anything else on television. The production is so elaborate that I sometimes wonder if people realize it’s all unfolding in real time.

It’s an honor to work on a show that is as demanding as it is fulfilling. We create compelling television that brings people together. People throw watch parties, and families across generations have something they can enjoy and talk about together. In a world where it’s increasingly easy to become isolated in our own individual experiences, Dancing with the Stars gives us a shared moment.

For this finale, viewers on both coasts will have the opportunity to watch and vote live, making that shared experience even more special.

When a dance transcends the steps, the technique, and the choreography, and somehow becomes greater than the sum of its parts, that’s the magic that makes it a ten for me. I can feel it. And that magic is what keeps bringing me back season after season.

This interview has been edited and condensed for clarity.

#6849. These Expert-Loved Ceramide Moisturizers Will Save Your Skin Barrier

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

These formulas are basically a cheat code to supple, hydrated, healthy skin.

If you shop as much as ELLE’s beauty-obsessed editors, you’re already pretty familiar with a few common skin care ingredients. Those with acne-prone skin know the benefits of salicylic acid, retinol, and mandelic acid all too well. Oily skin types typically reach for non-comedogenic formulas, while dry skin drinks up hyaluronic acid. Still, if there’s one ingredient all skin types can use in a routine, it’s ceramides. New to that world? Let us help you get more familiar.

“Ceramides are fats, or lipids, that your skin makes on its own,” explains Dr. Anne Han. While you might not have heard of them, think of ceramides as your skin’s security guard. “They make up about half of the lipids in the stratum corneum, the outermost layer of skin. I like the ‘brick and mortar’ picture: skin cells are the bricks, and ceramides, along with cholesterol and fatty acids, are the mortar that holds them together. That mortar keeps water in and keeps irritants, allergens, and microbes out,” she adds. When your skin becomes particularly dry and crackly, this could be a sign that it needs a ceramide boost.

“When ceramide levels drop, the barrier gets ‘leaky.’ Water evaporates more easily, and skin turns dry, tight, flaky, itchy, and more reactive. That happens with eczema, as we age, in cold, dry weather, and with overwashing or harsh actives. Ceramides in a moisturizer help top up what the skin is missing,” Dr. Han shares. Not all skin care products offer instant gratification, but adding a ceramide moisturizer to your lineup can boost moisture levels “sometimes within a day of the first use, with more benefit after a few weeks of steady use,” Dr. Han suggests, adding that “studies show ceramide moisturizers raise skin hydration and cut water loss” and “work best alongside cholesterol and fatty acids, the other two parts of the skin’s natural lipid mix, not on their own.”

All in all, every skin type can benefit from a ceramide moisturizer for an instant boost of moisture and overall improvement in skin over time. If your skin is in need of a tune-up, the list below, featuring derm- and ELLE editor-approved picks, is the perfect place to start. Shop every type of formula, from K-beauty staples to splurge-worthy finds.

This fan-favorite CeraVe cream has reached cult status for good reason. As with most formulas from the brand, this moisturizing tub is packed with ceramides and hyaluronic acid, which lock in and attract moisture, respectively.

Key ingredients: Hyaluronic acid, ceramides

Expert review: “[This has] several skin-identical ceramides with glycerin, is fragrance-free, works for face and body, and is affordable.”—Dr. Anne Han, MD

The powerhouse trio in this formula plays key roles in restoring and balancing the skin: ceramides protect, niacinamide addresses discoloration, and glycerin provides moisture. Together, they leave your skin deeply nourished and supple. Take it a step further with the SPF 30 version for even more protection.

Key ingredients: Prebiotic thermal water, ceramides, niacinamide, glycerin

Expert review: “It contains ceramides plus niacinamide, which can calm redness.”—Dr. Han

While a hydrated, moisturized barrier can improve the look of fine lines and wrinkles, this SkinCeuticals formula is designed to deliver a healthy moisture boost. With vitamin E, lipids, essential oils, and more, skin becomes smoother, suppler, and plumper with every use.

Key ingredients: Ceramides, vitamin E, essential oils

Expert review: “This has the full barrier-lipid trio, which is helpful for aging skin that recovers more slowly.”—AH

Packed with everything we love about K-beauty formulas—lightweight, hydrating, soothing—this barrier cream seeps into the skin like butter, improving its elasticity and suppleness.

Key ingredients: Ceramides, fatty acids, and cholesterol

ELLE editor review: “Believe it or not, my foray into Korean skin care came through Aestura’s products. In the dead of winter, when my semi-oily, acne-prone skin needed reprieve, I received a gift set with the brand’s bestsellers and instantly fell for this cream. My skin felt so hydrated and conditioned, and it even helped my makeup look more glowy (not greasy).”—Nerisha Penrose, beauty commerce editor

You’ve likely seen a TikTok video of someone applying this milky solution to their skin during a skin care routine or makeup tutorial. The barrier-strengthening “glazing milk” gives you an instant glow while working overtime to soothe and quench parched skin.

Customer review: “Oh how I love this essence. I usually use a thinner/clear essence but I am now a fan of this milky one. It gives an instant boost of hydration and helps with serum absorption.”

Medik8’s formulas are as straightforward as they come, with ceramides showing up in this moisturizer and its nighttime counterpart. In this composition, the main ingredient is combined with hyaluronic acid and peptides to protect stressed skin and visibly plump stressed skin over time.

Key ingredients: Ceramides, prebiotic peptides, hyaluronic acid

Customer review: “My skin feels great when I use it. Light enough for my oily skin but truly moisturizing, unlike others I’ve tried over the years which were either too light or too heavy.”

As the name suggests, the results are, in fact, instant. Think of this moisturizer as youthfulness in a bottle; it’s packed with moisturizing glycerin and urea, plus squalane, peptides, and ceramides for firming.

Key ingredients: Peptide blend (Dipalmityl hydroxylproline and hexapeptide-12), squalane, ceramides, glycerin, urea, and hyaluronic acid

ELLE editor review: “Look into my travel toiletry bag, and you’ll find about two of these tubes rolled down to their last squeeze—that’s just how much I love this cream. My skin feels instantly moisturized and supple, but looks even better when I wake up.”—NP

There’s power in numbers, so when shopping for an effective ceramide moisturizer, Dr. Han advises looking for one that combines similar ingredients. “Look for ceramides together with cholesterol and fatty acids, which mimic the skin’s own lipid mix. Ceramides used alone are less effective at barrier repair,” she says. Plus, look for formulas with “skin-identical” ceramides, which look like “ceramide NP, AP, or EOP (older names include ceramide 1, 3, and 6-II)” on labels.

Furthermore, you’ll want a moisturizer packed with “humectants, like glycerin or hyaluronic acid [which] pull water in” or “occlusives, like petrolatum or dimethicone to seal [moisture] in; niacinamide can help skin make more ofits own ceramides,” Dr. Han adds. Other important markers to look for include “fragrance-free” to avoid irritating sensitive or eczema-prone skin; for acne-prone skin, “non-comedogenic” products are great because they don’t clog pores.

“They do different jobs, so it isn’t really either/or. Ceramides are barrier ingredients and help the skin hold water and resist irritation, and that is what makes something a moisturizer,” Dr. Han explains. Meanwhile, peptides—skin care’s buzziest ingredient—are touted for their anti-aging properties. “They are meant to signal the skin to make more collagen or to soften the look of fine lines. If your main concern is dryness, sensitivity, redness, or eczema-prone skin, start with ceramides. The science behind them is more direct, since you are replacing a lipid your barrier actually lacks. If your barrier is in good shape and you want something for fine lines, a peptide product is a reasonable, gentle add-on,” she adds.

For this piece, beauty commerce editor Nerisha Penrose consulted a board-certified dermatologist to learn about the benefits of ceramides in skin care and which products are worth the investment.

#6847. Amber Glenn Reveals She Was Drug Tested Ahead of ‘Dancing With the Stars’ Finale

“Third time they’ve tested me this season, by the way.”

Amber Glenn and Pasha Pashkov came in second during an absolutely incredible Dancing With the Stars finale, and their flawless freestyle is even more impressive knowing how little sleep Amber’s running on. The Olympian told Variety that she was drug tested the night before the finale, saying, “I actually had...you saw the drug testing show up last night. Yeah, so didn’t get much sleep. Third time they’ve tested me this season, by the way, guys, what’s happening? So, I’m excited to get some rest.”

At this point you’re probably wondering if Dancing With the Stars is drug testing contestants, which, nope! As Variety notes in their Twitter caption, Amber is referring to being tested by USADA (United States Anti-Doping Agency), which is the “national anti-doping organization (NADO) in the United States for Olympic, Paralympic, Pan American, and Parapan American sport.”

As their website puts it, they’re “charged with managing the anti-doping program, including in-competition and out-of-competition testing, results management processes, drug reference resources, and athlete education for all United States Olympic & Paralympic Committee (USOPC) recognized sport national governing bodies, their athletes, and events.”

Since Amber is currently training for the U.S. Figure Skating Championships (taking place in Utah this January), she’s being tested by the USADA like all athletes. Which might come as a surprise to everyone who didn’t realize Amber’s been juggling training with DWTS!

Her coach, Damon Allen, explained to KRDO “She's been balancing dance on a rigorous schedule with that, as well as, being able to continue training, getting ready for nationals in January. It's been tiring. But she knows how to recover, and she's got a regimen, and she knows how to take care of her body, and figure out how to be at the top of her game at all times, whether it's in the dance arena or back on the ice.”

#6843. How the Holy Is Revered and Corrupted in The Body

Netflix’s buzzy new show, created by Quinn Shephard, examines guilt, God, and control through the eyes of teen girls attending a Catholic high school

I wanted nothing more than to see the Holy Spirit. One day after church, my grandfather described the moment he first believed was witnessing the Holy Spirit: a soothing, cleansing sensation in which God, in some way, possessed and reassured your mind. Divine intervention felt attainable for a moment. At first, I, too, hoped to discover serenity, an especially enticing thought since my parents were far less religious. But over time, that belief twisted in my preteen mind, instilling instead a fear that every misstep might damn me. To be viewed unfavorably by God—as a kid—is a terrifying thought. And it lingers.

I soon let go of the institutionalized beliefs. Yet, a longing to believe in something greater—especially when facing the unknown—never fully left me. I still desired to find spiritual ground, one free from this all-consuming, stress-fueling shame. This friction between spiritual surrender and self-actualization is the beating heart of Quinn Shephard’s The Body. Beneath a relentless downpour of religious guilt and an insatiable appetite for forgiveness, the students at St. Mark’s Catholic High School battle their inner demons as they discern what they want for themselves versus what the world expects of them.

Shephard and co-showrunner Gina Gammell thrust the audience into an age of Razor flip phones, AOL instant messaging, and Juicy Couture tracksuits, all set to a 2000s soundtrack featuring Kylie Minogue and No Doubt. Its barbed-edged storytelling draws comparisons to Cruel Intentions and Pretty Little Liars, with a bewitching touch of Jennifer’s Body or The Crucible. Here, girl teenagedom traverses suppressed sapphic love and the systemic weaponization of religious guilt—an incisive critique biting enough to solicit a petition with over 120,000 people pushing to cancel it. This coming-of-age series emerges from the dual nature of what we hold sacred: its capacity for reverence and its vulnerability to corruption.

Catholic school scares me. Teenagers scare me more, especially Lisbeth (Geena Meszaros), the hyper-religious, manipulative student at the heart of the story. At first, The Body introduces us to Lisbeth as the quintessential “weird kid,” socially ostracized because of her eyebrow-less face and her freakishly religious family. (She evokes Carrie in Carrie.) But as The Body unfolds, Lisbeth pulls—or rather, tugs—at the web of guilt ensnaring the school’s dance team, the Lions, concerning the near-death of their new teammate, Grace (Sofia Wylie). Grace fell into a coma shortly after the girls pretended to sacrifice her at a Halloween party, popping ecstasy pills they referred to as the “body of Satan.” This hazing ritual is all in good fun until Grace’s heart actually stops … if only briefly.

The aftermath casts a shadow of guilt over everything—especially for Anya (Kristina Bogic), queen bee and dance team captain, who moved Grace’s unconscious body back to her bedroom with Lisbeth’s help. Soon after, Anya begins to see visions of Grace in her dreams. The moment she confesses this to the group, the other girls start confirming visions of their comatose friend. This uncanny groupthink preys on the girls with something to hide, who carry shame with them wherever they go. Anya, for instance, feels deep contrition for her romantic feelings for her best friend Elise (Sarah Boustany), whereas Elise more boldly embraces her queer sexuality. Elise faces the world with a steady confidence, shielding her from spiritual panic—and its subsequent manipulation.

The same isn’t true for most of her teammates, all of whom seek solace in the intangible, in God and the Holy Spirit, in the saintly visions of Grace. Grace then becomes a symbol of the sacred, a story that inspires people and therefore can be corrupted to control others. The girls’ desperate need for forgiveness makes them vulnerable to Lisbeth’s manipulation. When the team goes on a getaway together, they take hallucinogenic mushrooms before deciding that they must purge evil spirits from Grace by way of an exorcism (led by Lisbeth, of course). Lisbeth controls this ritual, forcing each girl to confess their sins. (She already knows them all after reading all their private messages.) Her feigned omniscience incites hysteria among the girls.

Shame clouds nearly all the girls’ minds. Maddie (Shirley Chen) wrestles with an eating disorder, some girls admit to premarital sex or impure thoughts, and Lisbeth attempts to squeeze out a confession from Elise about her feelings for Anya. The Body runs the gamut of teenage girlhood, but Shephard distills these insecurities into a single critique of control: how the holy—the fear of God—can exploit people, corrupt what we see, and shape what we believe. In episode 6, “Star Quality,” mass hysteria breaks out in the town, with people associated with the church now believing to see visions and revere Grace as a saintly figure. It’s driven by an innate desire to be seen—to see something—and a desperate desire to be part of something larger.

This same desire, to witness something collectively transcendent, forged the foundation of my spiritual beliefs. It also scared me enough to run from them. A connection to God came with fear of hellfire and the shame of sin. In this way, forgiveness felt like something I received out of desperation, rather than salvation. The Body studies the dangers of this unmitigated belief, of a doctrine designed to parse out the unknown. But by whose authority? Shephard’s story reminds us that the storyteller—the pastor or the manipulative teenage girl—controls the things we see and the things we may believe.

#6842. Why These Very Unexpected Royals Are at Risk of Having Their Titles Stripped by Prince William

Prince William reportedly wants to “liberate” them.

Prince William has made no secret of his plans to “change” the royal family when he becomes king.

Slimming down the monarchy (no, not with GLP-1s, but rather cutting the number of working royals) and reducing “ridiculous” protocols are just some of the stark ways that the royal family will be deeply different if the Prince of Wales gets his way. But while some of William’s proposed changes seem logical, others have come as a surprise to royal insiders.

Chatting to Radar Online about his new book, Tom Sykes said that friends of William revealed he plans to scrap his children’s titles in the name of “liberation.”

According to the author of Dukes of Hazard: Harry, Andrew and Edward VIII: The Spares Who Shook the Throne, while Prince George will inherit the throne, William is keen for Princess Charlotte and Prince Louis to have other options. In fact, by taking away their HRH titles, William hopes that his kids can pursue careers unrelated to royal life.

“He’s gone and looked under the bonnet and is now making really difficult decisions and changing an organization that hasn’t been changed for a very long time,” another insider told Radar Online.

But if William’s plan goes ahead, it wouldn’t be the first time that members of the royal family went without their HRH titles. Both Princess Anne and Prince Edward opted out of giving their children royal titles, and the former Prince Andrew was shockingly stripped of his just last year.

Previously speaking about the decision to not bestow her children with a fancy title, Prince Edward’s wife, Sophie Wessex, told The Sunday Times, “We try to bring them up with the understanding they are very likely to have to work for a living.”

The Duchess of Edinburgh went on, “Hence, we made the decision not to use HRH titles.”

#6841. The 25 Best Cocktails for Thanksgiving

With a whiskey warmer, spiced sour, or comforting classic in your hand, you can get through any awkward family gathering.

Are you ready for your annual awkward family gathering? Thanksgiving is finally here, and if thinking about safe topics to discuss with all three generations of your family is making you break out in hives, then rest assured we’ve got some recipes to give you the liquid courage to face it.

You’ve got the turkey basted and roasting in the oven. You’ve got your grandmother’s perfect mac and cheese. You’ve got mashed potatoes—the best. And you’ve got store-bought pie from your slacker cousin. But, wait, do you know what you’ll be drinking for the most important feast of the year? We promise: There’s something for everyone at your dinner table on this curated list of cocktails.

Does Grandpa only abide by the saying, “If it’s brown, drink it down?” We’ve got a few whiskey drinks that will keep him on ice. Did your aunt who drinks only French reds run through the bottle she brought already? There’s a sangria recipe that’ll have her too busy sipping to snip. Need a warm cup of something strong while you “take the dog for a walk”? There’s an Irish coffee that’ll keep your wits about you. So hurry up and add these cocktail recipes to your Turkey Day menu.

Here’s a whiskey sour that makes your fall-flavor dreams come true.

Add ingredients to a shaker and shake without ice. Pour into a glass and garnish with grated nutmeg and a dried apple slice.

This twist on the old-fashioned adds an autumnal flavor that will keep this classic feeling fresh.

Combine all ingredients into a mixing glass with ice. Stir until chilled and diluted. Strain into a rocks glass with a large cube. Garnish with an orange peel studded with cloves.

The delicious spice that goes into a pie doesn’t have to be just for lattes or baked goods.

Cover the rim of a stemless wineglass in simple syrup. Dip in cinnamon sugar and add ice to the glass. Pour in pumpkin-spice liqueur and Josh Cellars sauvignon blanc. Stir, then top off the glass with seltzer.

This three-ingredient cocktail is easy to whip up but will taste like it was complicated and time-consuming.

Mix cider and cinnamon whiskey in a Champagne flute. Top with Champagne, then stir lightly until incorporated. Garnish with an apple slice.

A sweet, fizzy sipper to enjoy before dinner.

Add all liquid ingredients to a stemless wineglass. Add ice. Stir to incorporate, then garnish.

A roasty, toasty espresso martini to enjoy after the big dinner.

Shake ingredients hard with ice until frothy, about 15 seconds. Double-strain into a chilled coupe or martini glass. Garnish with coffee beans, nutmeg, and cinnamon.

This sumptuous hot cocktail makes a great Thanksgiving treat.

In a mug, dissolve the sugar cubes in a little hot water. Add rum and butter. Fill the mug with hot water. Sprinkle nutmeg on top.

The margarita isn’t just a summer drink anymore.

In a cocktail shaker, combine all the ingredients with ice and shake. Rim a glass with cinnamon sugar and serve over rocks. Garnish with slices of lime and orange.

You need to keep the booze flowing to keep the mood light and the conversation going. So mix together this sangria recipe that’ll satiate any apple-cider lover.

Place apples, orange, and cloves in a large pitcher. Drizzle honey over the fruit, then add in apple cider, rum, ginger beer, and white wine. Stir to mix. Top with ice and let chill for 20 minutes before serving. Serve in glasses, including the chunks of fruit.

Whoever invented pouring bourbon into a cup of tea, we want to give you a big hug.

Boil water in a teapot. Steep star anise pod or tea bag inside. Add bourbon, honey, lemon, and Tiki bitters to a mug. Stir. Serve in glass mugs with a spoon and the teapot. To enjoy, pour tea over cocktail base to taste and stir.

This aperitivo is the perfect refreshment before you start serving those heavy Thanksgiving dishes.

Pour Campari and sweet vermouth into a highball glass filled with ice. Top with club soda. Garnish with an orange twist.

A balanced spin on the Manhattan that uses Scotch whisky, this cocktail will impress any fan of brown liquor.

Stir the Scotch, vermouth, and bitters well with cracked ice. Strain into a chilled cocktail glass. Garnish with a lemon twist.

Simple enough to whip up in under five minutes, this cocktail has enough Scotch to knock Grandpa under the table.

Stir the Scotch and amaretto well with cracked ice in a mixing glass. Strain over fresh ice into an old-fashioned glass.

If you’re a big fan of cranberry sauce (the one that comes in a can), you can cure your cranberry craving with this vodka cocktail.

Shake ingredients in a shaker with cracked ice. Strain into a chilled cocktail glass, then garnish with lime.

This green-tinged cocktail tastes fresh and slightly tart, but it’s boozy enough to chill you out after that heavy holiday meal.

Add all ingredients into a shaker. Shake well with cracked ice. Strain into a coupe glass. Garnish with a lime wedge and Luxardo cherries.

The Black Russian is the espresso martini’s cooler, more laid-back cousin. It’s so simple to make and yet deliciously complex in taste.

Stir ingredients with ice in a mixing glass. Strain into an old-fashioned glass over fresh ice.

Sometimes you don’t want to have to choose between two options, because you want to have it all! And you can have it both ways, with this gin-and-vodka martini, the vesper.

Stir ingredients briskly with ice in a mixing tin until very cold. Strain into a chilled cocktail glass. Garnish with a large, thin-cut lemon peel.

Why not turn your holiday gathering into an international experience? Incorporate this Russian classic into your cocktail rotation.

Squeeze lime juice into a copper mug (or a Collins glass) and drop in the spent hull. Add ice cubes, then pour in the vodka and fill with cold ginger beer. Serve with a stirring rod.

This rum cocktail will bring back fond memories of warmer weather.

Fill a tall glass with ice cubes. Add rum. Pour in ginger beer and lime juice. Stir with a barspoon, then garnish with a lime wedge.

Holidays are synonymous with Champagne, so pop open a bottle of bubbly and pour out one of these delicious cocktails.

Stir rum, lime juice, and honey thoroughly with cracked ice in a chilled cocktail shaker. Pour unstrained into a Collins glass. Top with Champagne.

#6836. The Shopbop Black Friday Deals Worth Adding to Your Cart

ELLE editors found the best deals—and the brands with better discounts elsewhere.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

You could spend hours scrolling through individual fashion brands’ Black Friday sales—or you could do your shopping in one convenient place: Shopbop. Now through December 1, the Amazon-adjacent retailer is offering 25 percent off most items on its site with the code HOLIDAY. Plus, Prime members get free one-day shipping.

More than 25,000 items are included in the promotion, ranging from celebrity-worn handbags to winter staples. The retailer is also offering limited-time deals on menswear, beauty, and home products that make for great holiday gifts. To help you make the most of it, we’ve highlighted Shopbop’s best Black Friday deals—and detailed which brands you can find deeper discounts on elsewhere.

Shopbop is offering the same 25 percent discount on Hailey Bieber’s versatile Freja Caroline bag as you’ll find on the New York-based label’s site. We also spotted Selena Gomez’s JW Pei Delphine bag for under $100. Staud and Tory Burch are offering slightly deeper discounts on their own sites, so we’d skip Shopbop’s deals this time around.

Polo Ralph Lauren Bellport North/South Tote

Rag & Bone is offering 25 percent off sitewide for Black Friday, so you’re safe to shop for the label’s viral sweatpant jeans on Shopbop. We’d skip discounted denim from Paige, Madewell, DL1961, Levi’s, Good American, Joe’s Jeans, and Frame, because those brands are offering deeper discounts on their own sites. That said, we’re seeing Shopbop deals on pairs that are still full price at AG, Agolde, Mother, and Citizens of Humanity.

Shopbop is offering the same Black Friday discount as Reformation, Vince, and Leset, so jump on any deals you see from those brands. We’d skip La Ligne sweaters, since they’re up to 50 percent off on the brand’s site during its only sale of the year.

Reformation Caspia Brushed Cashmere Cardigan

Sold Out NYC Signature Cashmere Crew Sweater

Shopbop is offering Black Friday deals on over 2,000 pairs of shoes, so it’s worth peeking at categories your closet is missing. While we’ll be quick to add Puma and Asics sneakers, Flabelus flats, and Aeyde boots to our carts, we’ll pass on Loeffler Randall and Larroudé while those brands are offering 40 percent off on their own sites.

Shopbop’s sale is a smart place to invest in winter coats. The retailer is offering deeper discounts on Halfdays puffers than the brand itself. Stock of suede and leather jackets is already running low, so finalize your cart fast if that’s what you’re after.

Lola blankets, Bala bangles, Emi Jay claw clips, and other reliable gift ideas are competitively priced at Shopbop’s massive Black Friday sale. Why not check a few people off your list while you’re browsing for yourself?

#6835. The 15 Most Thoughtful Personalized Gifts

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

These will make shopping easier than ever this year.

It might only be November, but the holidays are just around the corner, and with Hanukkah starting early this year on December 4, I’m already collecting gifts for everyone I love. Getting ahead of the curve has its perks: fewer frantic shipping fees, for one, and enough time to find something that feels genuinely special.

The easiest way to make a gift special? Get it personalized. Because who wants to tear through wrapping paper only to find the same thing everyone else is getting this season? Go the custom route, and even a classic gift feels like it was made just for them (because, well, it was).

Think engraved ID bracelets and signet rings for the guys, birthstone bracelets and sparkly initial pendants for the women in your life, and personalized sports merch for the fan whose team is a whole personality trait. Add sentimental photo albums full of family memories and a few delightfully quirky surprises they’ll never see coming, and you’ve got a list worth getting a head start on. Here are some of our favorite personalized gift ideas for the holidays this year.

Know someone who takes great pleasure in writing hand-written letters? Make their next correspondence even more exciting with personalized writing paper from Smythson. Customize everything from the size and color of the paper, the text and motif at the top of the paper, and the tissue lining and border on the envelopes.

Engraved jewelry is one of the classiest personalized gifts you can get for someone, especially when it’s simple and refined like this ID chain bracelet from Miansai. I got this bracelet for my boyfriend with his name engraved on it for the holidays last year, and he wears it pretty much every day.

A personalized jersey is a no-brainer for anyone with a favorite sports team. The Fanatics custom shop has merch for NFL, MLB, NBA, WNBA, NHL, MLS, and even college teams, so it really has something for every kind of sports fan. And it’s not just jerseys—there’s also custom drinkware, bags, accessories, and more.

Even if your giftee isn’t super into astrology, they’ll appreciate a candle that’s just for them (or, well, anyone that shares their birthday). If they are into astrology, they’ll love all the fun facts about their birthday on the candle’s label, including their Zodiac sign, element, constellation, tarot card, and more. And all you have to know is their birthday, so it’s a pretty easy gift.

Speaking of birthdays, birthstone jewelry is another classic gift that’s personalized by design. And all you have to know is their birth month to get it right. Brilliant Earth has a great selection of birthstone jewelry, from pendant necklaces and colorful earrings to gemstone tennis bracelets and rings. You can even add multiple birthstones to this eternity lab diamond ring, so you can personalize it with the birthstones of everyone in your family.

For the one who spends their weekends on the golf course: They’ll truly impress their golf buddies with a box of personalized golf balls. And it comes with tees, a divot tool, and poker-chip ball markers so they have everything they need.

If you have the time and want to do something really special, order one of these luxury lighters from S.T. Dupont and get it engraved in person at a local engraving shop or jewelry repair store. The extra effort will show, and your giftee will have something to carry with them wherever they go for years to come.

You’ll have fun playing around with all the features in Ralph Lauren’s online custom hat maker—we certainly have. Choose the color and add a name, logo, or monogram, and the result will be a one-of-a-kind baseball cap your giftee will wear all the time.

Don’t just print out a photo and call it a day; get it printed on glass for something truly unique. Upload your photo to Fracture and personalize it even more with colorful borders, templates, frames, and stands.

Ever gone into a comic book store and seen the walls of Funko POP action figures from every franchise you can imagine? Having cute action figures of all your favorite Star Wars characters is great, but imagine how cool it would be to give someone an action figure of themself. With Funko POP, you can do just that—and you’ll have fun building it. You can even get a pair of Funko POP figures, so you can include yourself in it, too.

If you’re looking for a romantic personalized gift for your partner, surprise them with a watercolor painting of the place where you had your first date or the spot where you got engaged. Whether it was a local coffee shop, beloved movie theater, or beautiful outdoor space, it’ll look good painted above your names.

In a world where everything is online and in the cloud, a physical photo album is all the more sentimental and special. Artifact Uprising makes putting together a photo album easy, and the result is high quality and beautiful. It’s something they’ll want to display on their coffee table for everyone to see.

Gifts based on birthdays is a common theme in this gift guide, but what can we say? It’s an easy way to personalize stuff. If they’re a history buff (or just love reading the news), they’ll enjoy flipping through this customized book of newspaper headlines from their birthday through the years.

You know that person who is absolutely obsessed with their dog or cat? (Okay, it’s me.) They’ll obsess over any gift that has to do with their beloved pet, like this custom sweatshirt embroidered with the face of their dog or cat. Just upload a photo of their pet (you can probably find one on their Facebook or Instagram) and choose a sweater color and size.

A customized L.L. Bean tote bag is something they’ll use all the time. It’s one of our favorite gifts of all time, and it’s an absolute classic.

#6832. The Biggest Spring 2027 Trends Celebrate Glamour and Individual Style

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Get ready to wear tuxedo pants to brunch and sport a boa for the office

The breakout trends from the Spring 2027 shows were all about personalization and styling, humanizing the process of getting dressed at a time when AI can generate nearly anything for you. From the clashing of statement pieces at Chanel, Dries Van Noten, and Valentino to the coming-out party of the bra at Dior and Altuzarra, the spring runways celebrated self-expression.

There was also a touch of glamour almost everywhere. Dressing for daytime took on a new sheen via the cummerbunds and opera pumps at Kallmeyer, the metallic gold at Saint Laurent, and the boas at Tory Burch. At Michael Rider’s Celine, utilitarian staples were reimagined in sophisticated silhouettes, while brands like C and Bode took classic straight-leg jeans and added embellishment in the form of sparkle and embroidery. This season was all about celebrating the joy of getting dressed, a principle we think should be applied year-round.

And lest we forget, this was the season of the skirt—so much so that Miuccia Prada and Raf Simons sent 63 skirt looks down the Prada runway in Milan. Some were embellished, some had sheer details, and almost all of them fell right below the knee.

Now that fashion month has wrapped and we’ve had some time to digest what we saw on the runways, we’re rounding up our nine favorite trends from the spring 2027 collections. And because March is still months away, we’ve shopped out some ways to get a jump on the looks now.

Mixed-and-matched looks were all over the runways. What felt new here was the against-the-grain pattern play, which felt like a message about personal style—an exhortation from designers to stop worrying about following the rules and lean into whatever makes you feel good.

This approach allows you to play with things that are already in your wardrobe. Invest in a sparkly skirt to pair with a checkered shirt you already own, or pair a new jacquard jacket with some animal-print boots already in your rotation. The key to this trend is making it your own.

Bode Asymmetrical Sequin-Embellished Fringed Skirt

Stripped-back had a whole new meaning this season! Designers quite literally loosened up a few buttons, exposing delicate bras worn underneath shirting. (Granted, some looks like those at Prada and Tom Ford had just a bra as the top, but we’re going to save that one for warmer months.) What’s so appealing about this look is the contrast between the sexy underpinnings and the polished separates. There’s nothing like a little lace to spice things up, and we are here for it!

Borrowing from the boys isn’t new in fashion. Surely I’m not the only one who loves a Winchester banker shirt with monogrammed initials? But the tuxedo dressing trend—which started on the fall 2026 runways and will be going strong all spring—feels fresh. Designers placed a huge emphasis on the waist (I call it “waistmaxxing”), with the focal point of many looks being the cummerbund. A tuxedo jacket, tailored pants, and opera pumps would create a chic head-to-toe approach, but these pieces look just as cool individually. I love tuxedo pants dressed down with a white T-shirt, for example.

We’ve seen neckerchiefs and bandanas trending in previous seasons, but in spring 2027, neckwear takes a decidedly glam turn with the rise of the feather boa. I loved how Tory Burch paired her boas with car coats and silky shirts with trousers, while Dior’s models wore them with extravagant blazers. Both made the case that these three-dimensional scarves should be worn during the day, which feels cool, unexpected, and very much in keeping with the season’s dressed-up turn.

The New Arrivals Ilkyaz Ozel Feather Scarf

Designers really did their duty this season for the uniform dressers out there. If you love a tonal neutral, there were plenty to choose from in updated silhouettes, from balloon-style cargo pants to work shirts with peplums. What made them feel extra special was the way they were accessorized—with bold, colorful heeled boots and a bright satin bag at Celine, or with neon sandal heels at Balenciaga.

JW Anderson Parachute Drawstring Barrel-Leg Track Cargo Pants

The standout jeans this spring are covered in decadent sequins, embroidery, and beading. How fun is that? The key when it comes to styling is keeping your favorite basics on deck and building a look around the jeans. Whether you pair your statement denim with a classic white shirt for work (as seen at Dolce & Gabbana and Bode) or take them for a spin around the town with an equally dazzling top (like at Conner Ives), these are always a good idea—full stop.

Stella McCartney Crystal Embellished Straight-Leg Jeans

Polo Ralph Lauren Embroidered Mid-Rise Straight-Leg Jeans

Ramy Brook Lea Beaded High-Rise Wide-Leg Crop Jeans

With the price of gold at an all-time high and the jewelry market turning to silver, it’s been a refreshing change of pace to see glowing golden hues on the runways. #6828. This $120 Dyson Airwrap Dupe Works Even Better on Fine, Thin Hair

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Admittedly, when the Dyson Airwrap came out, I wasn’t entirely convinced it was meant for my hair type. I watched all the reviews: People with long, luscious hair achieve, well, long, luscious hair. My bob haircut (and fine strands) could never get hairstyles like that. Then, I became a beauty editor with access to all the fancy beauty devices, and I finally tried it. Within weeks, it replaced all of my hair tools. I could smooth my ends, give myself a ‘90s blowout, add waves, dry my hair quickly—all with one device. And it’s significantly less damaging too.

But since it was released, a zillion Dyson Airwrap dupes have come out, offering up similar results for a fraction of the price. Because that’s the catch: The Airwrap is great, but it’s a whopping $650 ($500 if you get it on sale right now for Black Friday). My curiosity is piqued every time a new multi-styler launches, but my go-to favorite for the past few years is the Ella Bella 6-in-1 Professional Hot Air Styler. And while it’s 20% off for Black Friday (at the lowest price I’ve seen all year), I’m breaking down exactly why it’s my favorite for fine, short hair.

With six attachments, this all-in-one air styling tool was made to replace virtually every other device in your cabinets. It features a hair dryer, two rotating curling irons (one for each side of your head), and three hair dryer brushes (one oval, one round, and one paddle for straightening). Like the Dyson Airwrap, this device uses hot air, not heated plates, to style, which helps reduce heat damage. The airflow on this thing is pretty damn powerful, reaching 110,000 RPM. And there are three temperature modes and two speed levels to help you tailor your styling to your hair type and needs (typically, the thicker your hair, the higher the temperature should be; and the same goes for speed). The device also boasts a “noise-reduction design,” so it sounds a little quieter than others.

My hair is truly as baby-fine as it comes and dries naturally straight. For this reason, it’s usually very soft and silky (love), but refuses to hold a style for longer than an hour or so (hate). I also color my hair (nope, not a natural redhead), so it’s damaged and prone to breakage. I’m growing out my bob haircut right now, so it’s a little past my clavicle—à la the clavi cut—and I have bangs that always require heat styling to look good. Currently, my go-to styles are typically a smooth blowout that makes my ends look less stringy in the bob grow-out period, or soft waves that add movement and texture. All this to say: The Ella Bella Styler has a lot of work to do (sorry!).

I received an initial rendering of this tool nearly a year ago, and it quickly became my go-to Dyson Airwrap dupe. In fact, I keep it at my desk at the office to style my hair after morning workout classes. The brand just released this slightly upgraded version that I hadn’t used yet, and upon first impression, it looks significantly higher quality. When I opened the carrying case and felt the tool, it’s lightweight (aka easy to hold for a long time if you are working with much more hair than I am) but looks luxe. The packaging is

Totally. At under $150 (a true fraction of the cost of the original Airwrap), the Ella Bella 6-in-1 tool is a pretty solid option if you’re looking to spend less money but still get a high-quality tool. I especially love how it works on my short, fine hair, but I could see someone with long hair falling in love with this too. I’ve already stashed mine in the green room at the office for midday touch-ups, and two people have asked if they could try it.

Beth Gillette is the beauty editor at Cosmopolitan with more than seven years of experience researching, writing, and editing hair stories.

#6825. Buck Mason Just Released a Very Cozy Collab with Sierra Designs

It’s the first team-up between the much-loved menswear brand and iconic outdoor supplier.

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

El Niño be damned. Winter is just around the corner, and even in a very weird year for weather, I can guarantee you this: There are cold days ahead. It pays to be prepared for them. And you could do a whole lot worse, preparation-wise, than the new collaboration between Buck Mason and Sierra Designs.

You probably know both brands already, but just in case you don’t, here’s a brief primer. Buck Mason, founded in 2013, makes the kind of cool-but-accessible menswear staples that you can build an entire wardrobe around. Sierra Designs is one of the OG American outdoors brands, founded in 1965 and responsible for iconic designs like the 60/40 mountain parka and all sorts of other equipment and clothing built for exploring nature. Both, handily enough, were born in California—and today they released their first-ever collaboration.

The new collection draws heavily from Sierra Designs’ ‘70s heyday, with reworked versions of an archival down jacket and vest serving as the stars of the show. Both are made from lightweight Japanese nylon and utilize a 600-fill-power, 80/20 blend of down and feathers for insulation. They’re complemented by a range of vintage-y tees, thermals, totes, and caps.

The overall vibe is easygoing and not too techy—exactly the sort of stuff you’ll want to wear in everyday life, not just on some windswept summit. Which is what makes it so damn good. There’s no shortage of high-performance winter gear out there, but much of it falls short on the style front. This is the sort of stuff that’ll do the job but won’t have you looking like you just finished a black-diamond run. Wear it with chinos and hiking boots or jeans and sneakers. To the coffee shop on a blustery morning or the park for a day out with the family. Or, yeah, even in the great outdoors. Just make sure to snag whatever you like soon, as Buck Mason’s collaborations have a nasty habit of selling out.

#6821. Burgundy Cat-Eye Nails Are the Most Mesmerizing Mani of the Season

There are a few nail colors I consider non-negotiable on my fingertips once fall rolls around: chocolate brown, black cherry, and, of course, burgundy. The latter is a rich, wine-like shade that’s classic, sophisticated, and something I find myself coming back to year after year. For 2026, the color has gotten a seriously mesmerizing makeover in the form of burgundy cat-eye nails. It’s not only a shimmery, dimensional manicure that’ll have you staring at your fingertips all day long, but it’s also a certified trend with searches for the look up more than 300 percent on Google. “Burgundy cat-eye nails have been one of my favorite looks to create behind the nail station,” says celebrity nail artist Erica De Los Santos.

Unlike a traditional glossy burgundy manicure, cat-eye nails use magnetic polish to create a light-catching effect that shifts and moves depending on the angle. Basically, it scratches a really specific itch in your brain that you’ll definitely become obsessed with (if you aren’t already). With the help of De Los Santos, I have all the deets on how to get burgundy cat-eye nails, plus plenty of photo inspo.

Burgundy cat-eye nails are exactly what they sound like: a moody and sophisticated rich, wine-red manicure created with polish that contains magnetic particles. This delivers a shifting shimmer that looks like velvet or polished gemstones—just enough sparkle to make these perfect for the holiday season.

“What makes this look so beautiful is how the depth and color shift change depending on the undertone of the base and the magnetic shade layered over it,” says De Los Santos. “The result is a multidimensional finish that catches the light beautifully, giving the nails a luxurious, almost velvet-like appearance.”

And the best part? There are so many ways to make the look your own. The magnetic effect can be customized depending on how much shimmer you want, and you can work a cat-eye into all types of nail art. “One of my favorite ways to wear burgundy cat-eye nails is as a solid color because the magnetic effect is already so beautiful on its own,” says De Los Santos. “But if you want to have a little fun with it, you can pair it with chrome starburst accents, playful polka dots, or even animal prints. The beauty of a burgundy cat-eye is how versatile it is.”

If you tell your nail artist you want a burgundy cat-eye manicure, they’ll have no problem picking the perfect deep red shade. However, you’ll need to specify whether you want a soft velvet cat-eye finish or a more defined cat-eye look. Bringing a reference photo is always helpful (gotcha covered below), since the placement and intensity of the shimmer can completely change the finished look.

After prep (“I always start by gently pushing back the cuticles, carefully removing excess dead cuticle, and shaping the nails”), De Los Santos applies one coat of a deep plum or burgundy gel polish and lets it cure.

Next, “I apply one coat of magnetic cat-eye gel, preferably in a shade similar to the base color, such as deep red, plum, or purple,” says De Los Santos. “Keeping the shades within the same color family really enhances the depth and makes the magnetic effect pop. Before curing, I use a magnet to create the desired light-reflecting effect.” She flash cures (a short, incomplete cure that’s a few seconds long) each nail as she applies the polish and magnet, and then fully cures all of them at the same time once every nail is painted. From there, leave your cat eye as-is or add chrome powder, designs, or any other nail art you love.

You can also create the look at home with magnetic nail polish and a compatible nail magnet (talk about an easy DIY nail art moment!). De Los Santos explains that gel gives you more time to manipulate the magnetic particles, but she also says there are lots of traditional polish options out there to get the cat-eye effect. With regular polish, prep is exactly the same, but the only thing that’s different is the application. “Apply a coat, immediately use your magnet while the polish is still wet, and move on to the next nail,” says De Los Santos (working with the polish while it’s still wet is key to get the magnetic particles to sit where you’d like them to). “Once you’ve completed all ten nails, you can repeat the process with a second coat if you’d like to build more depth and dimension.” Finish with the appropriate top coat.

Personally, I can’t think of a better manicure for the holiday party season. Check out some inspo pics below.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmo. She’s covered and tested all things nails, from the coolest new polish colors to cuticle care and nail art trends.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan and was previously the beauty assistant at Good Housekeeping, Woman's Day, and Prevention.
