#6819. 27 Charming Christmas Towns That Look Like a Real-Life Holiday Movie

Think twinkling lights, towering trees, and Santa-themed bike rides.

The holiday season is in full swing, with many halls and houses already decked to impress in garland and fairy lights. But as you wrap gifts, prepare to host, and begin marathoning holiday movies, it can feel like the joyous season is coming to an end much too soon. We can't blame you if you're still wanting more from this merry time of year, and we've come up with a solution. There are plenty of beautiful Christmas towns across the country to fill your heart with peace, joy, and nostalgia—you just have to know where to look.

These towns are more than just beautifully decorated—they’re truly immersive, offering experiences from Santa-themed bike rides to orchestrated light shows and winter bar crawls. Below, we've rounded up the 27 most charming Christmas towns from all over the country, so no matter where you live, you'll be close enough to find a bit of extra cheer this year.

This picturesque mountain town serves as the perfect holiday backdrop for more reasons than just the ever-present blanket of fluffy white snow. Each year, the valley is transformed into a Christmas town with seasonal theatrical performances, Gunnison’s Night of Lights, a tree lighting ceremony, and even an ice sculpting event.

Ever since the formation of this city on Christmas Eve in 1741, Bethlehem, Pennsylvania, has been rooted in the holiday spirit. From Christmas markets to historical tours to horse-drawn carriage rides, there's no doubt this town won't feel magical. And make sure to stop by the historic Sayre Mansion, pictured here, to check out their elegant holiday decor.

Annapolis is known as one of the best cities during the holiday season thanks to its winter weather and historic downtown. If you're lucky enough to visit this Christmas town, make sure you spend time bargain shopping until midnight, checking out the spectacular light display in Sandy Point State Park, and viewing the Yacht Club Light Parade.

Chicago is exceptionally beautiful during the Christmas time. From the seemingly endless strings of lights to the Christkindlmarket, which is free to enter and so charming, the Windy City has many offerings during the holiday season, making it one of the best Christmas towns to visit. Plus, you don't want to miss ice skating or any Christmas-themed museum exhibits.

If you want to do something Christmas-y almost every single day of December, then Newport, Rhode Island, might just be the place to go. To create the perfect coastal Christmas, this town offers a month-long celebration that's been going strong for 54 years. From holiday bike rides to Medieval festivals to winter afternoon teas, there's so much to do in this Christmas town.

You'll feel as if you've traveled back to Charles Dickens's time when you walk through Franklin's historic Christmas festival. Characters from A Christmas Carol and Oliver Twist come to life throughout the weekend-long festivities. Roam the Victorian Village and try Victorian-era Christmas treats like sugar plums while listening to carolers.

A classic ski town, Park City offers Christmas markets, festive decorations, and a Christmas Eve parade lit by torches—plus spectacular views of the mountains are around every corner.

In the heart of Virginia horse country, you'll find one of the most festive Christmas towns. Cheer on the town's hunting hounds while sipping hard cider and wander the streets in search of vintage Christmas ornaments.

During Kennebunkport's annual Christmas Prelude, the town puts on a very New England celebration: You can watch Santa Claus arrive by lobster boat, go on seashore holiday trolley rides, and enjoy wine tastings and lobster bakes. Not to mention, the town is covered in Christmas lights and garlands that make it the perfect festive photo backdrop.

For a dreamy and unique experience, head to Santa Fe. One of the most anticipated celebrations is the Canyon Road Farolito Walk, which is when thousands of farolitos—luminaries made of paper bags, sand, and tea candles—cast a glow on the gardens and old buildings that line the road. You also can get your white Christmas fix by visiting Ski Santa Fe.

As one of the oldest towns in the country, Portsmouth is a charming New England destination that celebrates the holidays with a Vintage Christmas Celebration. Visit the historic village of Strawberry Banke or wander around the charming shopping districts in this historic Christmas town.

Get a Hallmark movie feel while experiencing a taste of Denmark in California's Solvang village, located in Santa Ynez Valley wine country. The destination hosts Julefest, a month-long holiday celebration with live entertainment, a parade, and a night market.

Koziar's Christmas Village in Bernville is one of the top attractions in Pennsylvania. With more than a million lights adorning buildings, barns, and the landscape—and a reflective lake that doubles the beauty—it's hard to beat the town's display.

This Bavarian-style village is charming any time of year! December festivities include a display of Santa costumes from all around the world, a gingerbread house competition, carolers, and plenty of photo ops.

Of course, New York City isn't a small Christmas town, but it is extra magical during the holidays. From the massive tree at Rockefeller Center to the various Christmas markets in the city parks with ice skating, shops, and more, you can't beat the Big Apple's holiday spirit.

Nicknamed the Lighted Poinsettia Capital of Texas, this town brings major holiday spirit every year. Visit during December when the lighted trail in Comanche Trail Park is aglow all month long. Check out Hotel Settles for its elegant Art Deco lodging.

During the holiday season, each home in Historic Williamsburg lights one candle, a tradition that dates back to the Revolutionary War era. The cobblestone streets and colonial holiday traditions such as caroling by torchlight make this destination a must, especially for history buffs.

This Christmas town stays decked out year-round, so if you're craving holiday spirit in July, you'll still find candy-cane posts, boughs of holly, and a Santa Claus house with live reindeer!

Christmas without the cold? We've got you covered! In addition to abundant sunshine and festive holiday decor, you'll also have plenty of history to explore in St. Augustine, America's oldest city.

Want to take a ride on the Polar Express? Visit Durango during the holidays. The historic mining town offers a Polar Express–inspired trip that your kiddos will flip over.

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.

#6818. The Inconvenient Truth About the Electricity AI Requires

AI companies are facing political backlash, but they’re staring down an even bigger problem as they look to take over the world.

He-Man liked to exclaim: “I have the power!” AI should be so lucky.

When it comes to the wielders of artificial intelligence, and the data centers needed to fuel it, language and numbers become inescapably extreme: trillions in investment, gigawatts of electricity, the extinction of the human race. It’s almost cartoonish, until you get down to some brass tacks: The power is not there to fuel these dreams of the would-be Masters of the Universe.

“The U.S. grid is not designed for large data-center loads,” says Anubhav Arora, senior director of global infrastructure at Fitch and a coauthor of a recent paper the ratings agency published on data centers. “Securing reliable, sufficient power is the single greatest bottleneck in data-center development,” the paper’s authors write. (Esquire and Fitch share the same parent company, Hearst.)

In the U.S.—which is by far the world leader in data centers—electricity demand from the facilities could reach up to 649 terawatt-hours (TWh) a year by 2030, according to a 2026 Berkeley Lab analysis, or roughly 12 percent of all U.S. electricity consumption. Powering New York City for an entire year requires one tenth that energy.

Meeting global demand for compute could require nearly $7 trillion in capital investment by 2030, including $5.2 trillion for AI-related data centers, according to McKinsey estimates. Estimates from Goldman Sachs Research say that Europe will need to invest more than $3.5 trillion on its grid in the next ten years to cope with increased demand. (China looks to be fine, but it is slow to the data-center boom.)

And the problem is not just a lack of juice. “The actual grid infrastructure . . . is also basically limited in terms of transmission capacity, and the interconnection process is a little bit outdated,” says Arora. That grid—the network of transmission lines, transformers, and power plants that create and distribute electricity to hundreds of millions of users—is constrained, and the interconnection backlog is massive. Put another way: Not only is the shop running low on milk, but the line to get inside is stretching around the block. “You could build a data center in one and a half to two years, but to get interconnection, it takes five to ten years,” Arora says.

According to Cleanview, a company that tracks U.S. power infrastructure, there are currently 1,454 operating data centers, as well as up to 578 under construction and 2,663 proposed. Companies like Alphabet, Meta, and Anthropic are scrambling. With that scramble, there is money to be made. PJM, the largest power-grid operator in the United States, has been trying to devise a new way to accommodate the data-center boom. Its proposed “Bring Your Own New Capacity” (or BYONC) framework would have allowed new data-center projects to buy energy in the form of a battery and add it to the grid. But in October, the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission rejected that portion of PJM’s proposal, sending the grid operator back to the drawing board.

Important here is what kind of data centers we’re talking about. Of the more than 3,200 data centers proposed globally, 800 of them are hyperscalers. These are the biggies. The campuses. Sprawling centers with square footage in the millions and power demands upwards of 100 MW (which could power 100,000 homes). Collectively, some estimates put the global power usage at 200 TWh annually. (That’s juice for 11 million homes.)

How can companies even think about the grid beginning to handle that demand? Increasingly, they aren’t. Many hyperscalers are looking to “bring your own power [BYOP],” says Jonathon Smith, associate director of Sustainable Finance Research at Fitch. In other words, build their own power plants. The problem here is twofold: First, very few people on the planet know how to build something like this. You could buy the biggest army the world has ever seen; try moving it without engineers. Second, it poses very real and very dangerous environmental threats.

Recently, the journal Nature Sustainability published a study revealing that the annual carbon dioxide emissions of the largest data centers in the U.S. are equal to that of a small country, like Paraguay or Denmark. And that study looked at data centers from 2024 and 2025. Data centers have exploded since then, with as much as a 190 percent increase from 2025 to 2026.

When companies like Anthropic say, “Don’t worry, we’re not even touching the grid,” it brings up a cartoon situation more akin to The Simpsons than a Saturday-morning serial. Picture it: a giant data center, clean, cool, not an emission in sight, just humming machines and maybe a security guard. But there’s a pipe going out the back, and if you follow it as it snakes around the corner, it connects to a behemoth power plant, burning enough natural gas to power a city, with plumes of smoke assaulting the sky above. Animated, but closer to the reality of BYOP.

“From an environmental-impact perspective, most of these developers are deploying natural-gas-based assets for power, which release GHG [greenhouse gas] emissions and air pollutants,” says Smith. The cleaner alternatives are renewables (unlikely), nuclear (terrifying), and batteries (no environmental picnic, either).

Among the environmental concerns—which inevitably bleed into the social and economic ones—are these centers’ use of water, which they need in great volume to keep those humming stacks of servers cool. While many experts point to these issues as overblown, especially as data centers look to closed-loop systems (there is an initial dump of water that gets cooled and reused), the reason you won’t stop hearing about water use is because 1) it’s concerning, and 2) water is inherently political and always has been. (Traditionally, we’ve needed the stuff to feed our animals, grow our crops, and, you know, live.) Go back to the early chapters of the Bible and what’s causing strife? Water. Go back even further and some of the first wars ever recorded—4,500 years ago in ancient Sumeria—are over the stuff. And data centers have become a fast-moving political football.

A recent Gallup poll indicates that 71 percent of Americans do not want an AI data center near where they live. Those are some very big numbers. It’s important to clarify that the data centers big tech wants to build near dense populations are for cloud computing, and they make up roughly 70 percent of all data centers. While centers for AI computing and AI training are catching up, these can be built farther away, for the simple reason that we’re happy to wait a few minutes for AI to do our taxes but not for the cloud to recombobulate an email or Instagram post.

That’s still a huge number of data centers that need to be near you and me, so what will we do about it? The politicians are listening. New York State has issued a moratorium. There’s legislation limiting the centers on the table in nine other states. Gina Hinojosa, who just pummeled Greg Abbott in the Governor's race in Texas, campaigned on promises of a moratorium for permitting and construction on new data centers. Norway, the Netherlands, and Denmark are leading the charge on moratoriums and other restrictions in Europe as governments catch up with popular opinion.

Moreover, back in the U.S.A., Donald Trump has touted a “deal” with big tech on data centers, the Ratepayer Protection Pledge, supposedly protecting grids from the biggies. But never has Tip O’Neill’s maxim that “All politics is local” been more salient. More than 500 localities are forming their own moratoriums, zoning freezes, and permit pauses. Even after the midterms, how and if to move forward with data centers is a debate for states, counties, cities, townships, and any other geographic entity that makes laws, collects taxes, and breathes collective air.

As the Democrats take over Congress and data centers become every pol’s favorite new cudgel, as the European Union and member states enact regulatory frameworks (often tied to renewable energy and climate), as elected officials turn away from AI’s once sunnily vague promises, something is becoming clear: It’s humans who have the power.

#6816. This Wood Finish Was Once Seen as “Outdated.” But Designers Now Say It's Coming Back.

You might regret ripping out those kitchen cabinets.

For a period of time in the late 1980s to early 2000s, it seemed virtually impossible to enter a house and not come across honey oak. That bright, orange-toned wood finish covered kitchen cabinets, bathroom vanities, even baseboard trim and furniture around the house.

“You’d see it everywhere—cabinetry, trim, stair railings, and floors. It was the go-to wood tone for builders and homeowners alike,” Cathleen Gruver of Gruver Cooley tells House Beautiful. “But by the early 2000s, it started to fall out of favor as people shifted toward cooler, more neutral interiors.”

However, there’s been a noticeable shift in the way people are wanting their interiors to look, and warmer spaces are definitely back in style. With this evolution, can we expect to see a revival of honey oak, as well? We asked a handful of designers if they think this golden wood finish is back, and how they would style it for modern-day interiors.

Elizabeth Bennett and Mallory Robins, the founders of interior design studio Kobel + Co, believe honey oak is back for two reasons: The first is that warm tones throughout the entirety of the home are on the rise; the second is that honey oak has a lot of nostalgic associations for people who grew up in the '80s and '90s and are now homeowners.

“We're seeing a resurgence of styles from this era not only in homes, but in fashion as well, as we harken back to a pre-digital era,” Bennett explains.

“It’s not necessarily a full-blown comeback, but I’ve definitely noticed a soft return—particularly in cabinetry, millwork details, and furniture," Gruver says. “Clients aren’t always asking for 'honey oak' by name, but they are gravitating toward natural wood tones that offer warmth and character.”

Before you take a sledgehammer to those original honey oak cabinets, reconsider this tone by styling it in more modern ways. Keep reading to see fresh ways that designers are working with honey oak in their new projects.

As homeowners gravitate more toward lived-in, inviting interiors, honey oak is a wonderful way to add warmth. Leave it to the professionals to find stylish ways to make this once-outdated wood tone look cool again.

Plenty of designers will tell you to mix wood tones in your home to prevent it from feeling one-note, and the same is encouraged when honey oak makes a big presence. When designer Adina Hall took on the project above, there was too much honey oak in the space for her team to replace or refinish it all, so they embraced it instead.

“We developed a custom stain for the new wood floors, a whitewashed French oak that created a neutral canvas for the structure without competing with the existing honey tone,” Hall says. “We added custom cabinetry finished in a taupe-tone ash veneer, again a neutral tone, and we leaned into crisp whites and some cool tones like sage green and indigo blue as accents to create a balanced palette.”

Heavy amounts of warm woods can overwhelm the eye; it’s important to build a restrained palette and introduce interest with cooler tones, wood stains, sculptural furniture, and contrasting materials. “The key is to give the honey oak a contemporary context in order to make it feel like an intentional material that delivers a punch of warmth in an otherwise cool and architecturally bold yet restrained palette.”

Whether you’re adding in fresh honey oak or you’re trying to work around what’s already in your home, one of the best ways to ensure it feels modern and not like a ‘90s Tuscan-inspired home is choosing an earthy color palette. Muted greens, warm whites, greiges, taupe, light blues—these are all beautiful hues that won’t add even more orange-ish warmth to your space.

“Instead of making honey oak the focus of the room, I let the beauty of the wood shine through the use of clean lines, black matte touches, natural stones, and modern lighting,” designer Nancy Dow of Sierra Living Concepts says. She’s fond of using this wood, and uses these shades to create a modern and fresh appearance.

Rather than “wood drenching” a home in a single tone (as was often seen in the early aughts), Robins explains that current homeowners are exercising more restraint with their finishes. “Rather than the primary finish for a house on trim, floors, and cabinetry, it's being used in more subtle ways—a cased good piece or a kitchen island alone,” she says.

One of the reasons honey oak fell out of style is because it is a very warm tone, and in the world of “millennial gray,” an orange-tinted kitchen or bathroom isn’t very appealing. However, designer Marli Jones of Rebel House design studio believes this can be fixed simply by creating a contrasting palette.

In the kitchen space above, Jones used only honey oak cabinetry in the scullery to contrast the light, bright main kitchen. “It adds a coziness to the scullery and envelops the space in warm, natural tones,” she says. “That same honey color travels across the countertop stone and into the tile backsplash.” The two rooms are connected by the wall of glass and steel, but the change in materiality from the white cabinets to the honey oak creates a dynamic transition. “Warm wood always adds a grounding layer.”

When adding a honey oak piece into your home, do so intentionally. Opt for a “stunning built-in, a custom island, or as an accent detail that brings texture and warmth,” Gruver says. To emphasize the styling, pair the honey oak piece with contemporary hardware or painted elements to help it feel fresh and curated.

While honey oak can work in any interior aesthetic, there are a few styles it looks best in, according to Gruver: updated farmhouse and cottagecore. Basically, “any space that leans into character, craft, and comfort,” she explains. However, she's also seen the tone used successfully in more modern, organic spaces where there's a large emphasis on natural materials and textures. It all depends on how it's balanced, which holds true for any wood tone. She adds, “When styled right, honey oak can absolutely feel current again.”

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.

#6810. Dakota Johnson Wore Cherry Cola Nails, So Now I’m Wearing Cherry Cola Nails

A syrupy upgrade to your typical burgundy mani.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

One of my guilty pleasures is sipping on a large vanilla cherry Coke at the movie theater. I’m really not a big soda drinker, but there’s just something about the combination of a fizzy, cherry-flavored soda, a bucket of popcorn, and a good movie that does it for me. And luckily for me, I’ve recently discovered another way to indulge: cherry cola nails. Yep, my go-to concession stand order is officially making its way to my fingertips, and naturally, I’m eating (or should I say drinking?) it up.

Dakota Johnson was also spotted wearing cherry cola nails earlier this month at a screening of Flesh Impact in Los Angeles, which only further fueled my need to get the look. Similar to syrup nails, these have a glossy finish to them, but are dark, rich, and reddish-brown, just like the classic soda. It’s basically the perfect marriage of two of my favorite fall nail colors, burgundy and chocolate brown. Ahead, everything you need to know about the trend, plus plenty of inspiration for your next nail appointment. Movie theater soda optional... but highly encouraged.

Think of a Cherry Coke: It’s dark brown with touches of red when the light hits just right. And that’s exactly the goal of the manicure version. “Cherry cola nails are the perfect blend of deep burgundy, rich chocolate brown, and a hint of red,” says celebrity nail artist Jacqueline Pham. “Think of that beautiful, dark cherry soda color that looks almost brown in certain lighting but has a cherry red undertone when the light hits.”

They’re also a great way to dress up your nails for the holiday season: “It’s a little sultry, and a fun alternative to your classic burgundy red manicure,” adds Pham. “You can play around with different shades of brown and burgundy and a slight change in either color can create a completely different cherry cola effect.”

Need some ideas of how exactly to incorporate the color onto your fingertips? Firstly, Pham suggests wearing them as a single-color mani—glossy and straightforward with a classic, high shine finish. “It’s timeless, elegant, and looks beautiful on both short and long nails,” she says. She also likes adding a little shimmer too, which you can do with the help of Essie’s Gel Couture Liquid Diamonds in Size Me Up or Color Street’s Nail Strips in Velvet Dahlia. If you’re craving some nail art, you could also do a twist on a classic French manicure with cherry cola tips over a sheer nude base.

First things first, you’ll want to reach for more than one bottle of nail polish. “The secret to achieving the perfect cherry cola shade is layering your colors rather than using just one,” says Pham. “I personally recommend starting with a rich chocolate brown as your first coat, followed by a deep burgundy as your second coat.” This mimics a cherry soda too, with a brown base and a hint of cherry flavor and color. “The brown creates a warm base, while the burgundy adds cherry tint,” explains Pham. After applying a base coat, she says to do “one thin coat of chocolate brown and one thin coat of burgundy, allowing each layer to dry.” After those two coats, she says to “finish with a high gloss top coat to give it that glassy, soda-like shine.”

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmo. She’s covered and tested all things nails, from the coolest new polish colors to cuticle care and nail art trends.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan and was previously the beauty assistant at Good Housekeeping, Woman's Day, and Prevention.

#6809. Everything but the Plane: Here’s How One Brand Is Bringing Glamour Back to Travel

PS’s private terminals are the epitome of real luxury.

Imagine a trip to the airport with no lines. No lines to check in, to drop your bag, to be told your first-class ticket is the wrong first-class ticket for the first-class lounge. Now add to that no interpersonal friction. There are no snappy desk workers, no turbo TSA employees, and no graveyard-shift customs agents that give you grief.

Airlines can solve only so many issues. These are terminal problems. PS, a Los Angeles–based company operating its own luxury private terminals, is solving them. The goal is to get you as close to the charter experience as possible, all while still flying commercial. If you ask around or sit in the common areas and eavesdrop long enough, you’ll likely hear something along the lines of “I usually fly private, but when I don’t, this is what I use.”

The company has locations in Atlanta, Dallas Fort Worth, Miami, and Los Angeles, and the next order of business is expansion to the airports its clients use most—business hubs, mostly. But it started at LAX in 2017, and that’s the perfect airport for something like this. The horseshoe design and the traffic it creates, the small terminals, and the paparazzi culture do not cater to the A-list and executive frequent fliers that use it as a home airport. PS offers a way around that with on-tarmac connection from your flight to its terminal and vice versa. A chauffeur pulls you straight off the jet bridge and into a luxury fleet vehicle; that partnership is currently with BMW. If you don’t have any checked luggage, that 7 Series can (for an additional price) drive you straight to your destination within 35 miles of the airport. So if you are sitting in row 1 of first class, that’s about 50 steps between you and the i7 that takes you to your hotel. Or it can take you back to the PS terminal, which is not a lounge but acts as an über-private lounge experience.

It ain’t cheap: $1,300 per head one-way for the Salon (a shared bar area), $4,950 for a private suite (essentially an airport hotel room with butler and spa service; this price is all-inclusive), and $1,650 for that PS Direct drive-you-straight-home service. There are discounts and perks if you opt for yearly membership, but this is all on top of your ticket, folks.

And it can lean comically luxurious, with Champagne and caviar offered every time you enter—like before my 8:00 a.m. departure, to which I’m not opposed but which caught me a little off guard. There’s the jet-set marketing—tagline: The “back door” to the airport—and meant-for-the-timeline branded cocktail napkins: “You’ve arrived.”

But once you accept that PS could not afford to excuse itself from the flex-driven airport exclusivity economy, you can really admire its hospitality-inspired finishes. Its raison d’être is to get you through the airport with as little friction as possible, but it stands out in its commitment to hospitality within those confines.

For example, interior design and artwork are curated. The centerpiece artworks of each location are done in collaboration with local artists, made to fit the location they are in. The crown jewel thus far is in Miami, where PS took over the old Pan Am headquarters and flight-training academy. The mid-century building is a Miami landmark, with geometric thermal walls acting as the facades and this pinkish sandy and limestone-heavy concrete throughout. Argentinian-born ceramicist Nina Surel matched this with tile-work water features and a ceramic mural at the center of the property. Design like this, small and mostly unnoticed in the grand scheme of things, is what turns your mind back to a golden age of travel. It’s not credit card points and moneymaking schemes—though, actually, you can buy any of the non-centerpiece artworks—just beauty for the sake of beauty.

On top of that, the food and beverage program is considered and regional. The blue-corn cornbread with honey and a ricotta spread that chef Kelsey Kirwin (chefs’ names are on the menus, an indication of the atmosphere) is making at the DFW location overtook my mother’s as my all-time favorite. I would consider the signature PS martini—with (guess what) caviar-stuffed olives—worthy of a place on any hot big-city cocktail menu. And all the coffees, snacks, and water options are head and shoulders above those of both the general-population terminals and the litany of airport lounges within them.

As far as service, yes, an agent handles your party’s check-in and keeps you abreast of delays so you don’t have to open an app—but that’s to be expected. It’s more in the interim. Servers don’t hover, they listen, and they’re backed by an extensive behind-the-scenes operation: Mention that you prefer coffee, not bubbles, before a morning flight? A note is made on your profile for the next time you visit. Only ever ask for sparkling water when you fly out of LAX? They’ll know that in Atlanta. The entire operation seems to run on Guidara’s Unreasonable Hospitality, the same way all modern restaurants do. If you’re leaving Miami and mention to a business partner that you forgot to try a Cubano (on menu) and a cafecito (off menu), both will likely be delivered to your table, compliments of the house.

But every time I start to get wowed by the service details, I remember the price point and the level of clientele. That should be expected.

Standard terminals are air-side, post-security, where everything (even a fancy and expensive airline lounge) is working within those confines. Since PS operates land-side, with security the last stage of the terminal experience, it has the flexibility to be more hospitable to guests. For example, if you book a private suite, you can fly in, use it for a business meeting, and fly out. Or your parents can accompany the family to departure, hang out in the suite, and squeeze every last second with the grandkids out of your visit. It is an airport experience actually designed around the ways we interact and what matters to us when we travel. That, more than the Champagne and caviar, is the definition of luxury.

#6806. Couples Need to Stop Being So Weird About GTA 6

Grand Theft Auto VI came out last Thursday, and somehow the most unbearable thing about it isn't the grown men playing the game—it's the couples.

After the release of the game, my feed became a nonstop slideshow of printed contracts, handmade gift baskets, and girlfriends narrating their boyfriends' disappearance into Vice City like it's a deployment. Couples posted printed agreements that grant the player 10 full days of uninterrupted access.

I'm not here to tell anyone not to play a video game. People should do what makes them happy. But why are we all being so weird about a video game?

Ten days off from the relationship, so a man can play a game. I'd like to meet the woman who has ever been granted a 10-day leave from her partner, her family, her job, and the emotional labor of every relationship in her life. Women don't get to just disappear to pursue their hobbies.

Some women in relationships are putting together “offerings” for their partner. One viral example involved a GTA-themed gift basket stocked with snacks and drinks for the player's sessions. Another woman posted a Target-run basket of her boyfriend's favorite snacks with the caption, "This is your sign to make your man a GTA 6 basket!"

No. It's not. When was the last time he made you a basket for one of your hobbies?

On the flip side, some men are gifting their partners a basket, with the idea that women need something in return—a little distraction to keep busy while he's busy. Women are not toddlers who need to be handed a snack and a coloring book while the grown-ups play. We have friends. We have jobs. We have entire lives that existed before his Rockstar account and will exist after it. We'll be fine. We might even enjoy the quiet!

It all begs the question: Why can't men just have hobbies like normal people? Women have hobbies all the time. We go to pilates, we read, we get absorbed in a show, and nobody drafts a legal document. Let’s keep up that energy. Just play the game and also make time for the rest of your life!

But it isn’t just about people finding the time to play the game. There’s separate discourse among couples about the relationship players maintain with Lucia, the main female character.

Online, some women have expressed feeling jealous of Lucia. Around the game's lead-up, users on TikTok and Instagram were claiming that boyfriends who nurture a virtual relationship with Lucia could be considered cheating. A YouGov poll of 4,000 adults found around 6 percent would consider dating in games like GTA VI to be infidelity. That's a generally small number, but it's still making headway on social media.

Lucia is a character. She is pixels. If your relationship can be undone by a woman who exists only to rob a gas station with your boyfriend, the problem was never Lucia.

To be fair, some women have leaned into the joke. One girlfriend gifted her boyfriend the game with a four-rule counter-contract that ended with the line "Lucia is not your real girlfriend." I respect the bit—but it still icks me out.

On the other hand, there have been people claiming that GTA 6 is teaching men to be better partners. I’d like to politely disagree. The theory goes that watching Jason treat Lucia well, and putting effort into that relationship on screen, will make players better boyfriends in real life.

I'm glad they're being attentive to Lucia. I mean that. It’s a nice change to see the video game encouraging men to be decent human beings (though the bar is low). But at the end of the day, they're buying flowers, fixing things up, and doing the emotional work for a woman who doesn't exist, while their actual girlfriends are across the room eating a basket of Swedish Fish.

Can we all get real for a second? What grown man needs a video game to teach him how to treat a partner? Showing up, communicating, and putting in effort are not secret skills only unlocked by a video game. I promise you your boyfriend is not learning valuable relationship skills playing GTA 6.

So to any couples out there being particularly weird about GTA 6: play the game, enjoy it, and please just be normal. Be a person with a hobby. It really isn't that deep.

Why the Holidays Are Better When You’re Single

What My P.I. Work Taught Me About Dating Men

#6803. How to Care for a Thanksgiving Cactus so It Blooms Right on Time

Here's what experts say you need to know about this low-maintenance plant.

Want an easy-care plant to brighten your home through fall and winter? Skip the usual mums and try a Thanksgiving cactus. Though it’s native to Brazil, this plant looks nothing like a desert cactus: Its flat, toothed stems end in pointed tips, and its vivid tubular flowers usually appear in late November. That timing is how it got its name. It’s related to Easter and Christmas cacti, which flower around their namesake holidays.

Thanksgiving cacti can grow outdoors in USDA Hardiness Zones 10–12, but they’re well suited to indoor life in any climate. Their bright flowers come in colors including red, pink, white, and yellow, adding a cheerful burst of color to a room.

Wondering how to help yours thrive? Here’s what to know about growing and caring for a Thanksgiving cactus.

The Thanksgiving cactus loves bright spots that aren't in direct sunlight. Too much sun can cause the plant to turn yellow rather than its beautiful green, so keeping it by a north- or east-facing window or even a shady south- or west-facing window is ideal. For your plant to produce the best flowers, it shouldn't receive more than 12 hours of light. It needs the darkness to produce blooms.

All holiday cacti require a bit more magnesium than other houseplants, so finding your Thanksgiving cactus a magnesium-rich fertilizer you can add to a well-draining soil is essential. The Home and Garden Information Center (HGIC) suggests supplementing its water with Epsom salts—try mixing in one teaspoon of Epsom salts per gallon of water. But avoid doing so during the week you add the fertilizer, which you should do once a month. Stop adding fertilizer in the late summer months for better flower production in the fall.

Thanksgiving cacti should be planted in a pot with drainage holes at the bottom since they won't thrive in soggy soil. Consider adding peat moss or sand to the pot to prevent pooling water around the roots. Thanksgiving cacti are root-bound, so there's no need to repot them for about two to three years.

The soil surrounding your Thanksgiving cactus should never be soaking wet or bone dry. However, the soil should be consistently moist and never fully dry to the touch. Holiday cacti are prone to root rot, so standing water is your main enemy. During the hot months, they can stand to be slightly underwatered.

Like most tropical plants, Thanksgiving cacti thrive in a humid, warm environment. However, the typical household temperature can keep a cactus very healthy too. The temperature shouldn't get above 80 degrees Fahrenheit or below 55 degrees, though ideally you should keep your cactus between 75 and 80 degrees during the day, according to the University of Wisconsin-Madison's Horticulture Department.

Pruning is an important task for plant lovers to keep any houseplant healthy, and Thanksgiving cacti are no different. To promote bud growth, cut the stems in the late spring or early summer, and make sure you have the right gardening tools for the job. Be sure to save the cut stems for propagation too!

If you want more of these tropical cacti around your house, maybe for a huge, flowering centerpiece for the holidays, use cut stems to produce more plants. HGIC suggests using stems with three to five phylloclades (or stem joints) on each to start. Set the segments aside for a day to dry out, then stick them about one inch deep into a pot with moist soil. Do this only in the spring and summer while the plant is actively growing, and avoid doing it in the winter once it has bloomed.

Because the Thanksgiving cactus is native to Brazil, you should try to mimic its natural environment as best you can to get it to bloom, which is near the end of the rainy season. The gardening department at Michigan State University Extension explains that for the buds to develop on your indoor plant, Thanksgiving cacti need at least 13 hours of total darkness each day, bright light when they're not covered up or hidden away, and a consistent temperature ranging between 55 and 65 degrees. You should start this process in late September or early October if you want blooms around the holiday season.

Once the plant has flowered for the season, then you can go back to taking care of it the way you normally would with the help of the guidelines written above.

According to PetMD, Thanksgiving cacti are not toxic to dogs and cats, unlike another holiday plant, the poinsettia. However, that doesn't mean you should let your furry friends munch on it. If large amounts are consumed, your pets can experience vomiting, diarrhea, and a decreased appetite.

#6802. Why Do We All Love a Small-Town Holiday Romance? Guarding Stars Might Have the Answer

The fantasy of falling in love in a small town has surprisingly little to do with the small town itself.

I’ll find any excuse to cozy up with a holiday rom-com, so when Guarding Stars dropped on Netflix, it was a no-brainer. Gossip Girl and Supernatural alumni Leighton Meester and Jared Padalecki are embracing one of my favorite tropes: the good old “small-town holiday romance.” Leighton’s character Hannah goes with Jared’s character Jack to his hometown pretending to be his girlfriend, while actually serving as his bodyguard. It got me thinking: Whether we’re die-hard city dwellers or seasoned travelers, why do we still love the home-for-the-holidays romance trope?

For me, the answer lies in how this specific genre taps into our own deep-rooted desire for escapism. We’ve seen these kinds of fake girlfriend or friends-to-lovers storylines play out a million times before, so their predictability is incredibly comforting to watch. We have, quite literally, seen this film before. And during the heightened chaos of the holiday season, it places viewers in the safe bubble many people crave.

Plus, the small-town setting in Guarding Stars is perfectly cozy. The two head to Jack’s obnoxiously large family home in Montana that’s been pristinely decorated in Nancy Meyers–inspired aesthetics. And much like in one of my favorite holiday movies, The Family Stone, the house itself is just part of the fantasy. The idea of being welcomed into a close-knit community with acceptance might be exactly what we dream of the holidays being like. Being loved by a family that, in the case of Hannah, is not even your own is really intoxicating.

Even the dating looks more fun. Hannah and Jack spend the whole movie dating their way around town, doing cutesy romantic things like attending the local rodeo, getting caught on a kiss cam, and mucking out horses together in the family stables. These wouldn’t be your go-to date locations in a more urban, city-based setting, so their dating experience feels playful and different from everyday life.

And Guarding Stars finds a way to elevate this trope. Hannah isn’t actively looking for a man when all of this unfolds. She’s just doing her job as a bodyguard when she inadvertently falls into a snowball of festive shenanigans. Hannah is doing fine on her own, but there’s something sweet about watching her let her guard down. Guarding Stars gently suggests that by Hannah making herself a little more vulnerable, she’s making room for more people to love and support her. As someone who tends to handle the hard stuff alone (eldest daughter syndrome, anyone?), that really resonated with me.

So often, this genre leans toward women going to the small town and being “fixed” or changed by the relationship they encounter. Their lives in the big city are unsatisfying, actually, and they really would rather work at the Christmas tree farm. That doesn’t happen here. She ends up back in New York City, and that’s where the final action set piece of the movie happens.

Guarding Stars shows that the small-town holiday romance trope can change and evolve without losing its festive magic, because it’s about comfort and connection. We can be cozy and keep our apartments in New York City, and I’m thankful for that this holiday season.

#6800. Geri Halliwell’s New “Dark Honey” Hair Is Winter’s Most Expensive-Looking Color Trend: How to Recreate It

Rich, warm, and glossy, the Spice Girl’s new shade is winter-ready

Few hair colors are as iconic and instantly nostalgic as Geri Halliwell's natural red. The hue, synonymous with her nickname “Ginger Spice,” has, of course, evolved over the years, from copper highlights woven through fiery red lengths to a full head of golden blond and lighter strawberry-blond tones.

For winter, Halliwell has swapped her signature brighter tones for a richer shade. Enter dark honey hair. "What I absolutely love about Geri’s dark honey hair is the richness and depth of the color. A beautiful chestnut base with ribbons of warm honey, caramel, and subtle amber runs through the lengths and ends. It’s a wonderful evolution of her signature warmth, but with a softer, more understated finish," says celebrity hairstylist and Remi Cachet expert Adam Reed.

At the premiere of Only Murders in London, Halliwell wore her fresh hair color in a soft side part with dressed-out waves, a combination Reed says helps different tones catch the light.

The rich, luminous color adds subtle dimension that doesn't focus on dramatic placement, but on healthy-looking hair. "To recreate the look, I’d ask your colorist to maintain that beautiful brunette depth through the roots, introducing delicately placed honey and caramel lights through the mid-lengths and ends. A personalized gloss can bring those tones together and create that beautifully reflective finish," he adds.

Alternatively, Reed recommends his “just a little filler” approach, using extensions as color tools placed strategically. This approach uses additional hair to enhance color, density, and movement, rather than focusing solely on length. "By carefully selecting complementary shades of chestnut, honey, and caramel, we can introduce tonal variation without necessarily having to chemically lighten the natural hair," he notes.

Whether you're looking for a complete hair refresh this winter or simply want a subtle way to enhance your natural color without fully committing to a new look, Halliwell's dark honey hair is the perfect inspiration.

#6798. The 20 Best Colognes to Give as Holiday Gifts

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Impress everyone on your list with these great scents.

Fragrance makes a fantastic holiday gift. It’s something they can wear every day, a good bottle lasts for ages, and it feels undeniably luxurious. The only catch? Dropping serious cash on a scent they don’t actually like would, frankly, suck. Luckily, we’re pretty obsessed with fragrance here at Esquire. We’ve smelled, tested, and worn more colognes than we can count—and we know which ones tend to be crowd-pleasers.

It helps to know what your giftee is into. Do they want something light and fresh they can wear year-round? Something dark and smoky for winter? Or are they new to cologne, meaning a beloved classic is the safest bet? (And we know the fan favorites by heart—as well as all the best new fragrances from the past year.) Whatever their taste (or level of fragrance expertise), we put our heads—and noses—together to find the best colognes to gift everyone on your list.

It’s just sexy. This fragrance is not one to go under the radar and escape people’s attention. From experience, it will draw people in from across the room to ask what you’re wearing. A little clichéd but very much tried and tested. It’s unabashedly smoky and resinous, which makes for a captivating cloud of mystery. —Alfonso Fernández Navas, senior style and market editor

It’s a classic for a reason. Aromatic and bold. I like to wear it for the important kind of evenings when I need an extra bit of refinement. —Mark Burger, social-media editor

Fulton & Roark makes so many delicious fragrances, but Ramble is one of my favorites. It’s inspired by the famous Central Park enclave of the same name, so it’s perfect for any NYC native. The notes of forest air, lavender, and lemon rind create a fresh, woody scent that will transport you to the wilderness. It’s sophisticated and lush, and it works for literally everyone. —Bellamy Richardson, associate commerce editor

A reliable fastball of a men’s going-out scent. Up top, it’s fresh and gets compliments without being an attention-grabbing club citrus fragrance. On the dry down, it’s deep and sophisticated without being too fashion-y and cerebral.

This would be my go-to for most guys. I’ve given bottles to my brother (divorced dad in his 40s) and his oldest son (just turned 21). Gets compliments in both age ranges. —Luke Guillory, commerce editor

A versatile scent that wears well practically any time of the year. The citrusy notes are potent but not overwhelming. You need only two sprays maximum for this to hit. —Eric Francisco, entertainment editor

Snif’s new Double Take cologne is one of our new favorite scents—and at $74, it’s a much more affordable option than some of the more luxury picks on this list. With juicy apricot and fresh orange blossom at the top, creamy sesame milk at the heart, and a classic woody base, it’s beloved by everyone in our office who’s tried it. —B.R.

After years of using Ralph Lauren Polo Gold Blend, my wife took me cologne shopping at Le Labo in the West Village, where we spent more than an hour trying out different fragrances. I ended up going with Baie 19, which brings an earthy and refreshing feeling. It paired well with my body smell, which is a huge factor. Le Labo also offers a huge discount with its refill program; you just have to bring your bottle back. —Mike Kim, digital design director

Woody, rosy, fresh—a bright and sophisticated scent I like to wear all day. Plus, if it’s good enough for Tom Holland, it’s good enough for me. —M.B.

Byredo Mojave Ghost smells effortless, and even my friends who don’t usually like scents always compliment it. The bottle design also makes it perfect to put on display—it looks so chic sitting on a desk or counter! —Emma Saxon, fashion assistant

Ranger Station is a little perfumer out of Nashville, and as such it’s got a lot of country-music references in the lineup. This one, done with the Waylon Jennings estate, embodies the man himself: gruff, masculine, old-fashioned.

It’s a curveball as a gift because it’s heavy on musk, leather, smoke, and spice. It smells like the Stetson Original my grandpa used. (It smells more like it than the modern Stetson Original does, funnily enough.) But that’s why I love the idea. This type of scent has been lost to time, so these days it’s really, really unique. —L.G.

If you know someone who can’t live without coffee, they’ll probably love a coffee-scented cologne. This one from Maison Margiela’s Replica line is one of our favorites thanks to its nice blend of sweet and spicy notes. It also strikes a perfect balance of light and fresh (from top notes of lemon, lavender, and spearmint) and rich gourmand (from notes of warm coffee, milk mousse, and sweet vanilla), so it’s not too heavy in either direction. —B.R.

Pretty much any Dolce & Gabbana cologne is a winner in our books, but The One for Men collection is a good bet for any guy. The eau de parfum is a woody, ambery fragrance centered around orange, sage, and tobacco, giving it a sophisticated overall vibe that works well at the office or on a date. —B.R.

I gravitate toward gifting warm, cozy scents and this one feels perfectly timed for the season. With notes of almond, rose, and vanilla, this powdery gourmand feels soft and intimate. A touch of honey adds some nostalgic sweetness. —Andrea Ríos, fashion assistant

This is a new favorite of mine I’ve been wearing every day. Iris Germanica and vanilla give it a floral sweetness that takes the edge of the manly musk typical of Legend. Plus, it’s hard to go wrong with anything served in a flask. —Bryn Gelbart, associate commerce editor

I really campaigned for this scent during our Grooming Awards earlier this year. It’s insanely wearable. Spice, florals, and citrus top and dry down hit in the warmer months. In the colder months, the warm, earthy heart stands out. It’s soft without being too femme, sturdy without the usual smoke and whiskey smells. I think the difficulty with this fragrance would be finding someone who it doesn’t jive with. —L.G.

Iris Silver Mist by Serge Lutens was first introduced in 1994, and there’s a reason this unisex scent remains so coveted. This fragrance blends the scent of moist, green earth with the light, powdery, and slightly metallic qualities of iris. Let this be your introduction to niche perfumery. —E.S.

If you know anyone who likes warm scents for the colder months, this new release might just be perfect. The notes of cognac and antique wood make me feel as though I’m sitting in an old home next to a roaring fire with a drink in hand. But it’s not overwhelming or cloying—just inviting. —Jonathan Evans, style director

I don’t wear scents on a daily basis, but whenever I spritz one on for a special occasion, it’s always Diptyque. The brand makes the most beautiful yet understated fragrances that are just right, never leaving too heavy of a cast or going unnoticeable. Lazulio is my latest go-to, a more natural scent that is a perfect blend of fresh and classic in a single bottle.

A gift from the brand always stands out thanks to its beautiful packaging. No matter what, the recipient will feel the luxury. Plus, I can bet they don’t have this scent yet. But if you’re not exactly sure what they like, you can play it safe with this discovery set of five scents. —Krista Jones, senior director, commerce and partnerships

One of my favorite scents. Versatile enough to move from day to night yet striking enough to leave an impression no matter the hour. The balance of bergamot, musk, neroli, and vetiver is masterful. The bottle—with tinges of Art Deco influence—is equally sharp. —M.B.

#6796. A Case for Being Single During the Holidays

valentine’s Day is the official holiday for couples—so why does the fall and winter holiday season feel like a love and relationship fest?

This time of year has literally been deemed “cuffing season”: You’re encouraged to take couples pictures at a pumpkin patch, bring a date to Thanksgiving, drink hot cocoa by the fireplace at Christmas, and have someone special to kiss at midnight on New Year’s Eve.

But even though we’ve been inundated with countless signs that this is the best time to couple up, I’m going to go out on a limb here and say that I actually think there’s no better time than the holiday season to be single.

“The holidays can actually be a great time for creating our own traditions and sense of self,” relationship expert Bethany Nicole tells Cosmopolitan. “Being single over the holidays gives us a much-needed time of rest, reflection, and recalibration.”

So instead of falling victim to the pressures of coupling up during the holidays, why not just lean into being single instead? You might even find yourself enjoying the holidays more. Why? Thought you’d never ask:

According to Newsweek, 65 percent of Americans admitted that they’re stressed about the amount of money they’ll spend during the holidays. And for 73 percent of them, this financial stress actually makes the holidays less enjoyable. Can you guess who people are spending most of this money on? That’s right: their children and their romantic partners.

Sure, you might still have to get your parents, siblings, and your weird aunt a holiday gift—but isn’t it satisfying knowing that you don’t have to budget a huge chunk of change for yet another person in your life? By staying single during the holidays, you’re actually keeping your pockets happier.

The holidays are a time to spend with loved ones. But with so many events centered around the holidays, you might be spending more of that precious time with someone you’re just getting to know instead of with your friends and family. Assuming you like your friends and family, this is not the best ratio!

When you’re single, you can spend the holiday season with whoever you want. You can go to endless friendsgivings without having to drag along a plus-one, you can eat Thanksgiving dinner comfortably with your family without having to check on someone else, and it’s actually really fun to go out on New Year’s Eve with your best friends.

On the other side of the coin, family time in general is not for the weak. I get that, because mine isn’t either. If you were in a relationship, you’d probably be panicking right now about how to explain to your partner that your aunt is really funny if you ignore that she hates your mother or that your dad salutes the turkey before he cuts into it. But when you don’t have a date to Thanksgiving, you can just enjoy the weird and disturbing quirks of your family without having to explain them away to your partner.

When you’re coupled up, you’re expected to be “selfless” and “generous.” Ew. When you’re single, on the other hand, you can just be your normal, selfish self—and I truly think that’s beautiful.

Someone with a partner would probably be expected to give up the last piece of the pumpkin pie or be giving with an extra big piece of turkey. But because you aren’t in a relationship, you can have as much pie as you want—without pretending like you’re not picking up the speed while eating so your partner doesn’t get more bites than you.

Being around your own family for the holidays is torture enough—imagine if you then had to add a partner’s family to the mix? Not just that, but you’d most likely have to pretend to like them, too. And even get to know them!

Meeting an S.O.’s friends and family is a lot of pressure. Even though the holidays are a time of joy and celebration, they’re also super stressful. And you just don’t need the added stress of trying to impress your boyfriend’s dad, who doesn’t believe women should be in leadership positions.

Sure, having a partner around the holidays is nice—doing romantic things with someone you care deeply about can really add to the experience. But if you really want a partner, you have the rest of the new year to try and find someone.

For now, I say save your money, hang out with your friends, and do what the holidays were intended for: eating pie until you’re sick.

#6795. James Talarico’s Political-Powerhouse Girlfriend, Brianna Menard, Is Also a Master Thrifter

The 2026 midterm elections brought a blue wave of record-breaking wins. Among the most monumental was James Talarico, who became the first Democrat to win a Senate seat in Texas since 1988.

But his historic win is not all the internet has been talking about. At his celebratory Election Night event in Austin, Texas, the 37-year-old senator-elect was joined by his girlfriend, Brianna Menard, who was previously his chief of staff. Days later, the two attended a University of Texas football game—to which Menard wore a corduroy minidress she thrifted the week before—and the moment went viral across social media. (Even better, Talarico’s mom was the one who shared the photo of the couple at the game that quickly got traction on X.)

Chalk it up to her political prowess, clear sense of style, or the fact that she looks like an Instagram baddie, but some fans have even gone as far as to declare the pair this generation’s John F. Kennedy and Jacqueline Kennedy.

The couple first met in 2022, when Menard joined the politician’s team as chief of staff, and their relationship eventually turned romantic. Menard has been by Talarico’s side throughout his Senate campaign, accompanying him to the polls on Election Day in November and later joining him onstage to celebrate his historic victory.

So, who is Brianna Menard? Ahead, everything to know about Talarico’s accomplished and stylish girlfriend.

Menard is a born-and-raised Texan. She attended and graduated from the University of Texas at Austin, where she studied government.

In 2022, Menard was hired as Talarico’s chief of staff. According to reports, the two didn’t begin dating until a year later, after which Menard left her role working for the politician. In August 2026, Talarico’s campaign confirmed the couple’s relationship. “Four years ago, after he and his then-chief of staff developed feelings for each other and started dating, she left the office to pursue other opportunities,” a Talarico campaign spokesman told the New York Times.

After leaving Talarico’s office, Menard worked on the campaign for Rep. Salman Bhojani, the first Muslim member of the Texas legislature, and eventually became his chief of staff.

Menard is currently the director of TEXPAC and political education at the Texas Medical Association. According to her bio on the organization’s site, before stepping into the role, she had a decade of experience working in the Texas Legislature and previously served as executive director of the Early Childhood Caucus of the Texas House, where she led bipartisan efforts to “advance early learning, childcare access, workforce development, and healthcare affordability.”

Menard’s bio also lists her as the Board Secretary for Wheatsville Food Co-op and a member of the City of Austin’s Early Childhood Council.

According to a now-deleted bio on the Wheatsville Food Co-Op site, where she currently serves as Board Secretary, Menard called herself a “foodie and a committed vegan” who loves cooking. In the welcome post, she also revealed her love of yoga, dancing, and cuddling her cat named Zia.

“I’ve spent my career serving in the Texas Legislature and working on political campaigns across the state with the goal of making Texas a healthier and more equitable home for all of us,” she wrote in the post.

Menard joined Talarico at the polls to vote early in the midterm elections. On November 3, she also joined Talarico’s Election Night event in Austin, where she congratulated the Senator-elect on stage following his historic win. The couple shared a sweet hug and kiss to celebrate.

#6792. Tom Hardy's 5 Most Stylish Characters, Ranked

From Inception's sharply-dressed con-man to the getaway driver in RocknRolla, here’s what you can learn from his on-screen wardrobe.

Tom Hardy doesn’t usually play the good guy. He’s been a forger, a fixer, a getaway driver, a rogue spy, and, in one of his more ambitious films, both halves of the most notorious twins in British crime (not that there’s much competition). Most of them are walking red flags, sure, but they’re red flags in great clothes.

That’s partly down to the costume designers, who’ve given Hardy everything from ’60s East End tailoring to Steve McQueen-inspired ’70s outerwear, but it’s also down to Hardy, who has a particular knack for making a wardrobe feel like his own. Put him in a Western shirt or three-piece and he’ll look equally at home in both (and you’ll want to steal the look, either way).

So, we’ve dug through the archives to rank his best-dressed roles of all time, and one of them’s actually on TV right now. Consider it your new watch list—just ignore the red flags.

Everyone remembers the spinning top and Joseph Gordon-Levitt’s three-piece, but the best-dressed man in Inception is the one who usually gets overlooked: Tom Hardy’s Eames. Costume designer Jeffrey Kurland imagined him as an expat from a Graham Greene novel, and every piece was made from scratch (even though half of it looks straight off a Dries Van Noten runway). Think batik-print shirts, collars spread over relaxed blazers, sharp trousers, and a wallet chain—basically clothes for a guy who spends too long sitting in the sun and definitely doesn’t wear SPF. Then there’s the watch: a gold tank-style antique Kurland picked up from the prop master’s collection—and he’s since forgotten the brand. Reddit’s watch sleuths are still on the case, 16 years and counting.

Legend gave its costume department a very important task: how do you tell Tom Hardy apart from Tom Hardy? Lead designer Caroline Harris and Soho tailor Chris Kerr solved it with buttons: Reggie, the charmer, wears single-breasted suits that feel safe and easy to trust. Ronnie, meanwhile, is the volatile one who gets three-piece double-breasted silhouettes with extra padding to make him look macho. On both sides, though, everything’s cut from vintage fabrics for a lived-in look straight from the hanger. Sixty years on, you could walk into any hotel bar dressed as either and nobody would bat an eye. Until you start talking business, that is.

Inspired by the real-world Cold War scandals that rocked British Intelligence, Tinker Tailor Soldier Spy follows the hunt for a Russian mole inside MI6 in 1973. So, as you might expect, it’s lots of men doing stuff in gray suits. Not Hardy’s Ricki Tarr, though. He’s the only agent with any personality, wardrobe-wise, all thanks to costume designer Jacqueline Durran. Her thinking? Steve McQueen on a mission: a shearling coat in Istanbul, a Baracuta Harrington in Paris, and not a standard suit in sight. The kind of menswear every guy on your FYP is wearing today—Hardy was just 15 years early.

In MobLand, Pierce Brosnan and Helen Mirren head up the Harrigans, an Irish crime family at war on several fronts—and no one knows it better than Hardy. He plays Harry Da Souza, the guy they call when things go wrong, which seems to happen fairly often. Costume designer Rebecca Hale gave him a wardrobe for the job: a battered wax jacket when he’s taking care of the Cotswolds estate, say, or a big overcoat when he’s doing business in the city. There’s one exception to the low-key menswear staples, though: his watch. In season one, he wore a solid gold Rolex GMT-Master II, which is reportedly the most valuable watch in the show. Pretty good for the hired help.

MobLand isn’t Hardy’s first Guy Ritchie gangster part. In RocknRolla, he’s Handsome Bob, the Wild Bunch’s getaway driver. And while everyone else looks like they’d had someone ‘”taken care of” before lunch, Hardy’s low-key menswear staples make him look more like the boy next door. A faded denim shirt. A classic bomber with jeans. A blue checked Western shirt, open just far enough. All simple pieces that let his charm do its thing, which it does when he sweet-talks a lawyer into revealing who’s been grassing to the police. Four years earlier, he played Clarkie in Layer Cake, another member of a London crime crew but one with a very different wardrobe: slicked hair, blazers, open shirts, brandy, and cigars. Similar JD, just with a very different wardrobe.

#6790. How to Look Great at the Airport

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Take inspiration from these celebrity travel outfits

Let’s face it—all too often, travel is not so glamorous. With flight cancellations, long TSA lines, and schlepping luggage, you may not feel chic throughout it all, but your travel look can still be. Peak holiday season is here, and some of the busiest travel days are still ahead; we drew inspiration from a few of our favorite celebrities to bring you the best travel outfits for women, making your next excursion all the more stylish.

Comfort is often top of mind, but that doesn’t mean you have to settle for just leggings. Katie Holmes showed how to wear a simple white button-down shirt and baggy patchwork jeans on a flight, adding comfortable soft loafers and a stylish houndstooth jacket—a cool spin on a relaxed travel outfit that still feels put together. Kendall Jenner went for something polished, wearing a silky cream matching set, while Naomi Watts sported a leather bomber jacket, T-shirt, wide-leg sweatpants, and bright-red sneakers, showing the many different directions you can take to fit your own personal style.

Ahead, shop the 9 best travel outfits for women that will ensure your journey is just as good as the looks you packed.

As Katie Holmes proves, you don’t always have to rely on leggings for the airport; instead, elevate your look with a white button-down shirt and baggy patchwork jeans. Styling those with a houndstooth jacket, gray cashmere knit, and soft leather loafers, you’ll look incredibly polished throughout your travel journey.

Guest in Residence Shrunken Cashmere Sweater

Everyone knows matching sets are the easiest wardrobe choice when traveling, but take yours a step further with a silky cream version, as Kendall Jenner did. For a little extra warmth, throw on your favorite wrap, cardigan, or coat.

Sometimes your tried-and-true capsule wardrobe essentials are all you need. Naomi Watts paired a leather bomber jacket with a gray T-shirt and coordinating wide-leg sweatpants for a relaxed take. Bright-red sneakers add a playful touch, balancing the neutrals. From curbside to the arrivals gate, you’ll be comfortable throughout the duration of your trip, even after a red-eye.

The Canadian tuxedo works just as well on the street as it does in the air. As a seasoned globe-trotter, Julianne Moore is constantly moving from one city to another and certainly knows how to look pulled together while doing so. Take notes from her minimalist look with a French twist. Layer a black suit with a striped Breton top, both easy foundation pieces you’re sure to wear throughout any journey. From there, keep accessories classic with optic white shoes and an oversize shopper.

When Alexa Chung steps out, we take notice. Here, she puts her signature spin on all the travel classics while traveling. A khaki trench coat, a lace-trimmed camisole, and straight-leg white jeans are all you need. By adding oversize black sunnies, leather slip-on shoes, and the iconic Boat and Tote bag by L.L.Bean, she brings a laid-back, cool sensibility to airport fashion.

Intimissimi Pretty Flowers Tank Top With Lace

Her straightforward tank top and loose wide-leg trousers demonstrate how comfort can be a priority without sacrificing style. Don’t forget to bring a long cashmere cardigan to ward off in-flight chills and an oversize travel tote bag to complete the look.

Reformation Katerina Lug Sole Chelsea Boots

When you’re looking to travel with a little more style, look to Mikey Madison for inspiration. By using a trench coat as her focal piece, she creates an effortless travel look by layering a sweater underneath and pairing it with slinky black pants. Suede flat slides, a bright handbag, and a trustworthy carry-on create the perfect combination for your next getaway.

Burberry Long Kensington Heritage Trench Coat

Banana Republic Lightweight Cashmere Crewneck Sweater

A clean leather belt and tortoise sunglasses are all you need to ace this look.

#6788. Amazon’s Black Friday MacBook Deals Are Pretty Impressive

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Considering the price of RAM, we’ll gladly take $500 off a new MacBook.

If there’s one Apple product line where the recent price hikes hit the hardest, it’s the MacBook. Rising RAM and storage costs have pushed laptop prices up across the board, which makes scoring a new MacBook on sale all the more mission critical.

As luck would have it, Black Friday deals on Apple tech have landed on Amazon, with up to 20 percent off MacBooks—including the brand-new M6 MacBook Pros on sale for the first time ever. Given that these prices have reached record highs lately, these discounts are certainly nothing to sneeze at. In fact, now that the MacBook pricing status quo has been reset, we’d say this is probably as low as MacBook prices will get these days.

Of course, not all MacBooks were created equal. While the standard MacBook Air is great for most, those who are planning on running hardcore software might benefit from a beefed-up MacBook Pro. And if budget is your primary concern, we’re of the opinion that the MacBook Neo is a worthy buy, even if it’s only $50 off right now.

The best MacBook Air deal you’ll find right now is this $300 discount on the 15-inch model. With this laptop, you’ll get Apple’s M5 chip in a fanless, 3.3-pound frame that handles heavy multitasking and light video editing without breaking a sweat. Base storage doubled to 512 GB this year—a great upgrade for storing more apps, photos, and files—and the 15.3-inch Liquid Retina display is sharp and vivid. You also get a 12 MP Center Stage camera that’s great on video calls, Wi-Fi 7 for fast downloads, and up to 18 hours of battery, enough for a busy workday and then some.

If you’d prefer a smaller laptop, 13-inch models are up to 19 percent off. They’ve got everything the 15-inch models have, just in a more portable package. The rule of thumb for choosing between a Pro and an Air? The slimmer Air won’t replace a MacBook Pro for serious creative work or gaming, but for school, business, and general use, it’s the one to get.

Apple 2026 MacBook Air 13-inch (M5, 16 GB RAM, 512 GB)

Apple 2026 MacBook Air 13-Inch (M5, 16 GB RAM, 1 TB)

Apple 2026 MacBook Air 15-inch (M5, 16 GB RAM, 1 TB)

Given that Apple just dropped a new slate of M6 MacBook Pros, in addition to the company’s first-ever touchscreen laptop, we were anticipating discounts on the old range of M5 models to clear out inventory. Sure, the best MacBook Pro deal you’ll find right now is $250 off the 14-inch M5 model—but, if you don’t mind paying just $50 more, you can scoop up the shiny new M6 on sale, complete with improved speed and boosted AI capabilities. It’s extremely rare for Apple to discount its latest laptop so soon after release, and even more rare for it to be $200 off.

Both the M5 and M6 models feature Liquid Retina XDR displays, up to 24 hours of battery, three Thunderbolt 4 ports, HDMI, an SD card slot, and up to 2 TB of storage.

Why go Pro over the Air? The GPU is fast, which makes a difference when editing video, working with high-res photos, or gaming, and the XDR screen is significantly brighter. If your daily workload has started becoming too much for the Air to handle, this is where you should look next. If you want the biggest, most powerful model possible, souped-up MacBook Pros with more RAM are up to $500 off.

Apple 2025 MacBook Pro 14-Inch (M5 Pro, 16 GB RAM, 1 TB)

Apple 2025 MacBook Pro 14-Inch (M5 Pro, 24 GB RAM, 1 TB)

Apple 2025 MacBook Pro 16-Inch (M5 Max, 36 GB RAM, 2 TB)

The MacBook Neo is Apple’s most affordable laptop, even with the recent price hike from $599 to $699. Right now, the Neo is $50 off, sitting smack in the middle between its debut price and post-increase price, at $649—making it far and away the cheapest MacBook.

The Neo is an easy recommendation for anyone looking for their first Mac. It runs on Apple’s A18 Pro chip and is surprisingly quick for browsing, sending email, and streaming video. The 13-inch Liquid Retina display looks great for the price, and the battery lasts a solid 16 hours. It also comes in four fun colors, with the yellow-green citrus being a fan favorite.

There are some trade-offs with going with Apple’s budget model, including storage that starts at 256 GB, just 8 GB of RAM, and no keyboard backlight. You also get only two USB-C ports, so getting a USB-C hub is a must if you plan to connect a lot of accessories. Spending an extra $100 gets you a Neo with 512 GB of storage and a Touch ID sensor for quick unlocking. For students or anyone who mostly sends emails and browses the Web, the Neo is hard to beat.

#6785. The Princess of Wales Swaps Her Classic Waves for a Romantic Boho Hairstyle: How to Recreate It

During her three-day tour of India, the Princess of Wales attended the 2026 Earthshot Prize Ceremony in Mumbai. Prince William founded the initiative in 2020, and it awards environmental solutions with grants to scale and advance their projects.

For the event, Kate wore an oxblood, floor-length Anarkali dress by designer Mahima Mahajan and paired it with extra-fluffy, voluminous bohemian curls pinned in a half-updo.

An elegant, extra-glossy wavy hairstyle has long been a beauty signature for the princess, often teamed with a side part, hats, or a polished ponytail. But for the green carpet in India, the royal opted for a much more dramatic style that matches the glamorous mood of her outfit, with tighter, cascading bohemian curls woven into a beautiful half-updo twist for a romantic finish.

"I would create these curls using a medium-sized tong," recommends Philipp Haug, co-founder of Haug London Haus salon. "Take relatively small sections and use the tongs with a twisting technique, curling one section away from the face and alternating with one section towards the face throughout. Slightly root stretch when you come to the top of the head instead of curling too close to the scalp."

To enhance the fluffiness and volume of the curls, shake them out with your fingers instead of combing them.

Your next special occasion hair inspiration, sorted.

#6780. Don’t Forget About Stocking Stuffers—Here Are 25 Easy Choices

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

It’s true that sometimes the appetizer is just as good as (or even better than) the entrée, and that certainly applies to holiday gifts. What the heck am I talking about? Stocking stuffers. Those bite-size delights you open up before the main course (those bigger boxes under the tree), if you will. And if you do it right, a good stocking stuffer can be the best surprise of the holiday season, so put in a little effort. Don’t just throw in a keychain and magnet and call it a day.

What makes a good stocking stuffer, you ask? Well, considering these gifts should be small enough to, you know, fit in a stocking (whether that’s what you’re really using them for or not), anything bigger than a water bottle is out of the question. But that leaves you with tons of fun options—think tiny tech gadgets, stylish accessories, skincare upgrades, and little luxuries that punch well above their weight. We rounded up the 25 best small gifts with big appeal that everyone will be excited to find in their stocking this holiday season.
